Here are 250 WAEC-standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations, generated from the provided textbook chapters.

---

### **Chapter 1: Introduction to History and Historiography**

1.  According to the text, the Greek word 'historia', from which 'history' is derived, means
    A) memory of the past
    B) inquiry, knowledge acquired by investigation
    C) the study of kings and empires
    D) a collection of dates and names

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states, "History (from Greek historia, meaning 'inquiry, knowledge acquired by investigation') is the study of the past..."

2.  Which branch of history focuses on the everyday lives and social structures of ordinary people?
    A) Political History
    B) Economic History
    C) Cultural History
    D) Social History

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text defines Social History as focusing on "the everyday lives, experiences, social structures, customs, and relationships of ordinary people in the past."

3.  A history textbook is an example of a
    A) primary source
    B) secondary source
    C) oral tradition
    D) material artifact

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists "History textbooks (like this one!)" as a prime example of a secondary source because they interpret and synthesize primary sources.

4.  The process of checking a historical source for its authenticity is known as
    A) internal criticism
    B) contextualization
    C) external criticism
    D) corroboration

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines External Criticism as the process of asking "Is the source what it claims to be? Is it genuine or a forgery/fabrication?" which is a test of authenticity.

5.  Which school of historiography emphasizes class struggle and economic factors as the primary drivers of historical change?
    A) Nationalist Historiography
    B) Feminist Historiography
    C) Marxist Historiography
    D) Revisionist Historiography

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that Marxist Historiography "Emphasizes economic factors, class struggle, and modes of production as the primary drivers of historical change."

6.  An original letter written by a soldier during a war is best described as
    A) a secondary source
    B) an oral tradition
    C) a primary source
    D) a historiographical review

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines primary sources as "Original, firsthand accounts, documents, or artifacts created by witnesses or participants during the historical period being studied." A soldier's letter fits this perfectly.

7.  Judging past events by present-day standards is a historical error known as
    A) contextualization
    B) anachronism
    C) historiography
    D) chronology

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text warns historians to "Avoid anachronism (judging past events or people by present-day standards, values, or knowledge that would not have been available to them)."

8.  Griots in West Africa are most associated with the preservation of
    A) written records
    B) archaeological evidence
    C) political treatises
    D) oral traditions

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that custodians of oral traditions are "often specific individuals or groups... (e.g., griots in West Africa, elders, court historians)."

9.  Which of the following is NOT listed as a reason to study history?
    A) Developing critical thinking skills
    B) Understanding the present
    C) Cultivating empathy
    D) Ensuring financial success

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text lists understanding the present, learning from the past, developing critical thinking skills, cultivating empathy, identity, and informed citizenship as reasons to study history. Financial success is not mentioned.

10. Feminist historiography primarily aims to
    A) focus on the achievements of national heroes
    B) analyze the role of warfare in history
    C) recover and analyze the historical experiences of women
    D) challenge the existence of objective historical truth

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explains that Feminist Historiography "aims to recover and analyze the historical experiences, roles, and contributions of women, which have often been overlooked."

11. The study of the writing of history itself is called
    A) Archaeology
    B) Historiography
    C) Chronology
    D) Paleography

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text defines Historiography as "The study of the writing of history; the history of historical thought and interpretation."

12. Comparing information from multiple sources to check for consistency is a method known as
    A) contextualization
    B) internal criticism
    C) synthesis
    D) corroboration

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Under the section on Internal Criticism, the text lists "Corroboration: Comparing information from multiple sources to check for consistency or discrepancies."

13. Afrocentric historiography primarily seeks to challenge
    A) Marxist interpretations
    B) Feminist perspectives
    C) Eurocentric biases
    D) Nationalist narratives

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that Afrocentric Historiography aims to "re-center historical narratives from the perspective of African peoples... challenging Eurocentric biases that have often marginalized or distorted African history."

14. The Annales School of historiography is known for its focus on
    A) political events and great leaders
    B) military strategy and technology
    C) long-term social and economic structures
    D) the inevitability of progress

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text briefly describes the Annales School as focusing "on long-term social and economic structures, mentalities, and everyday life, rather than just political events."

15. According to the text, what is a major limitation of written records as historical sources?
    A) They are always objective and unbiased
    B) They are difficult to transport
    C) They often reflect the views of the literate or ruling classes
    D) They cannot be distorted over time

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists a limitation of written records as: "Often reflect the views of the literate or ruling classes; may be biased or propagandistic."

### **Chapter 2: Early Civilizations in Africa**

16. The Nok civilization is most famous for its
    A) bronze sculptures
    B) massive pyramids
    C) terracotta sculptures
    D) gold and salt trade

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text clearly states that the Nok Civilization is "Best known for its distinctive terracotta sculptures."

17. The 'lost-wax' or 'cire perdue' method was a sophisticated technique used in the art of
    A) Nok and Ancient Egypt
    B) Ife and Benin
    C) Kanem-Bornu and Oyo
    D) Ancient Egypt and Ife

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that Ife was renowned for sculptures in "bronze (using lost-wax casting method - cire perdue)" and that Benin art was known for its "technical mastery (lost-wax casting)."

18. In Ancient Egypt, the ruler who was considered a divine king and intermediary between gods and people was the
    A) Vizier
    B) Scribe
    C) Pharaoh
    D) Nomarch

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines Pharaohs as "The rulers of ancient Egypt. Considered divine or semi-divine kings, intermediaries between gods and people."

19. The two most important commodities in the Trans-Saharan trade were
    A) gold and salt
    B) ivory and spices
    C) cloth and horses
    D) slaves and kola nuts

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text emphasizes that gold from West Africa and salt from the Sahara were the key commodities, stating salt was "Often traded ounce for ounce with gold."

20. The political organization of the Oyo Empire included a powerful council of seven chiefs known as the
    A) Sarki
    B) Ogboni
    C) Alaafin
    D) Oyo Mesi

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Oyo Empire's political organization, stating the Oyo Mesi was a "Council of seven principal non-royal chiefs" that acted as a check on the Alaafin's power.

21. The ancient civilization of Ife is considered the ancestral and spiritual homeland of the
    A) Edo people
    B) Hausa people
    C) Yoruba people
    D) Fulani people

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Ife as being located in "Southwestern Nigeria, considered the ancestral and spiritual homeland of the Yoruba people."

22. What was the primary purpose of the great pyramids of Ancient Egypt?
    A) To serve as temples for public worship
    B) To act as tombs for the pharaohs
    C) To function as astronomical observatories
    D) To be administrative centers for the government

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that the pyramids were "primarily built as tombs for pharaohs and their consorts" due to the belief in the afterlife.

23. The spread of which religion was greatly facilitated by the Trans-Saharan trade routes?
    A) Christianity
    B) Judaism
    C) Islam
    D) Hinduism

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text highlights as a major impact of the trade: "Spread of Islam: Arab and Berber traders from North Africa were predominantly Muslim. They brought Islam to West Africa along trade routes."

24. Usman dan Fodio's jihad in the early 19th century led to the establishment of the
    A) Oyo Empire
    B) Kanem-Bornu Empire
    C) Benin Kingdom
    D) Sokoto Caliphate

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text explains that Usman dan Fodio "led a jihad that overthrew most Hausa rulers and established the Sokoto Caliphate."

25. The Benin Expedition of 1897 by the British resulted in the
    A) creation of the Benin Kingdom
    B) looting of many Benin bronzes
    C) introduction of the lost-wax casting method
    D) establishment of the Oba's political power

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes, "Many Benin bronzes were looted by the British during the Benin Expedition of 1897 and are now in museums worldwide."

26. The economic life of Ancient Egypt was heavily dependent on the
    A) Sahara Desert
    B) Red Sea
    C) Nile River
    D) Mediterranean Sea

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text quotes Herodotus, "Egypt is the gift of the Nile," and explains that the civilization was "heavily dependent on the Nile River for water, fertile silt... agriculture, and transportation."

27. The Berber and Tuareg nomads played a crucial role in the Trans-Saharan trade as
    A) miners of gold
    B) farmers of dates
    C) rulers of the Sudanic empires
    D) traders, guides, and controllers of oases

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text states, "Berber and Tuareg nomads of the Sahara played crucial roles as traders, guides, and controllers of oases and routes."

28. The long-ruling Sefuwa dynasty was associated with the
    A) Oyo Empire
    B) Benin Kingdom
    C) Kanem-Bornu Empire
    D) Hausa States

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text notes that the Kanem-Bornu Empire was "Ruled by the Mai (king) of the Sefuwa dynasty (one of Africa's longest ruling dynasties)."

29. The political power of the Alaafin of Oyo was checked by the Oyo Mesi and the
    A) Ogboni Society
    B) Sarki
    C) Mai
    D) Vizier

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that the Ogboni Society, "a powerful secret society of elders and notables," acted as a "further check on Alaafin and Oyo Mesi."

30. Which civilization is known for being one of the earliest in West Africa to produce iron?
    A) Ife
    B) Benin
    C) Nok
    D) Oyo

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Nok as "One of the earliest known complex societies in West Africa to produce iron" and mentions evidence of early iron smelting technology.

31. Ma'at in Ancient Egyptian belief represented the concept of
    A) the afterlife
    B) divine kingship
    C) cosmic order, justice, and harmony
    D) polytheistic worship

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that the Pharaoh was responsible for maintaining "Ma'at (cosmic order, justice, harmony)" and that the concept of Ma'at was "fundamental" to their beliefs.

32. The city-states of Kano and Katsina were major commercial centers within
    A) the Oyo Empire
    B) the Benin Kingdom
    C) Kanem-Bornu
    D) Hausaland

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Kano and Katsina as Hausa states and notes they "were major commercial centers" participating in the Trans-Saharan trade.

33. Which West African civilization is renowned for its highly naturalistic and idealized bronze and terracotta heads?
    A) Nok
    B) Ife
    C) Songhai
    D) Ghana

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text describes Ife art as "highly naturalistic and sophisticated sculptures in terracotta, bronze... Idealized but lifelike human heads and figures."

34. The decline of the Trans-Saharan trade was partly caused by the
    A) rise of the Roman Empire
    B) rise of European maritime trade along the West African coast
    C) discovery of gold in the Americas
    D) spread of Christianity in North Africa

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists one reason for the decline as "the rise of European maritime trade along the West African coast (shifting trade focus to Atlantic)."

35. The political system of the Hausa states before the 19th-century jihad was primarily based on
    A) a single, unified empire
    B) a council of elders with no king
    C) independent city-states ruled by a Sarki
    D) a nomadic confederation

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the political organization of the Hausa states as "City-states, each ruled by a king (Sarki)."

### **Chapter 3: The Transatlantic and Trans-Saharan Slave Trades**

36. The second leg of the Triangular Trade, involving the transportation of enslaved Africans to the Americas, was known as the
    A) Middle Passage
    B) Final Leg
    C) Columbian Exchange
    D) Atlantic Voyage

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly identifies the "Second Leg (Middle Passage - Africa to Americas)" where enslaved Africans were forcibly transported.

37. A major cause for the shift to African slave labor on American plantations was the
    A) refusal of Europeans to work
    B) decimation of Native American populations by disease
    C) high cost of indentured servants
    D) voluntary migration of Africans

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that "Native American populations were decimated by European diseases and brutal exploitation, making them an insufficient labor source."

38. Which of the following was a major social impact of the slave trade on West African societies?
    A) A long period of peace and stability
    B) Increased warfare and political instability
    C) A significant increase in population
    D) The rapid development of local industries

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Increased Warfare and Instability" as a key social impact, stating that the demand for slaves "fueled inter-ethnic and inter-state wars, raids, and general insecurity."

39. The movement to end the slave trade and slavery was known as
    A) colonialism
    B) abolitionism
    C) nationalism
    D) pan-Africanism

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The chapter title is "Abolition Movements in Europe and America," and the text discusses the "movement to abolish the slave trade."

40. The first European nation to abolish its slave trade was
    A) Great Britain
    B) France
    C) Portugal
    D) Denmark

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text states, "Denmark (1803, effective): First European nation to abolish its slave trade."

41. The dispersal of millions of people of African descent throughout the Americas is known as the
    A) African Diaspora
    B) Columbian Exchange
    C) Great Migration
    D) Scramble for Africa

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text defines The African Diaspora as "The dispersal of millions of people of African descent... throughout the Americas... as a result of the slave trade."

42. The Haitian Revolution, a major slave revolt, was led by
    A) William Wilberforce
    B) Olaudah Equiano
    C) Toussaint Louverture
    D) Nnamdi Azikiwe

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text mentions the "Haitian Revolution 1791-1804, led by Toussaint Louverture," as a key event that inspired abolitionists.

43. Which religious group was among the earliest and most consistent opponents of slavery?
    A) Anglicans
    B) Catholics
    C) Methodists
    D) Quakers

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies that "Quakers (Society of Friends) were among the earliest and most consistent opponents of slavery on moral and religious grounds."

44. The last country in the Americas to abolish slavery, in 1888, was
    A) the United States
    B) Cuba
    C) Brazil
    D) Jamaica

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states, "Brazil (1888): Last country in the Americas to abolish slavery."

45. Syncretic religions like Vodou, Santería, and Candomblé are examples of
    A) the economic legacy of slavery
    B) political resistance to slavery
    C) cultural exchange and adaptation in the African diaspora
    D) the failure of African culture to survive the Middle Passage

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists these religions as examples of "Syncretic religions combining African spiritual beliefs with Christianity" as part of the cultural contributions of the African diaspora.

46. Before the large-scale external trades, slavery in some African societies often resulted from
    A) racial ideology
    B) warfare, debt, or punishment for crimes
    C) the demand for plantation labor
    D) European economic policies

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains that prior to external trades, the causes for internal slavery included "warfare (prisoners of war), debt, punishment for crimes, famine."

47. The Eric Williams thesis connects the abolition of the slave trade to
    A) purely moral and humanitarian concerns
    B) the declining profitability of slave-based economies
    C) the success of slave revolts
    D) pressure from the United Nations

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes, "Some historians argue that the declining profitability of slave-based plantation economies... made abolition more palatable to economic interests (Eric Williams thesis)."

48. European traders most commonly obtained slaves in Africa by
    A) conducting large-scale raids themselves
    B) establishing plantations in Africa
    C) trading goods like guns and textiles with African intermediaries
    D) kidnapping the entire population of coastal towns

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text clarifies that "More commonly, Europeans established trading posts... and obtained slaves from African rulers, chiefs, and merchants in exchange for European goods."

49. One of the main arguments of those who claim slavery contributed to the underdevelopment of Africa is that it
    A) introduced new technologies to the continent
    B) extracted Africa's most valuable resource, human labor
    C) led to the creation of larger, more stable states
    D) promoted balanced internal economic growth

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text suggests one argument is that the trade "contributed to the underdevelopment of Africa by extracting its most valuable resource (human labor)."

50. The British Royal Navy played a significant role after 1807 in
    A) promoting the slave trade
    B) suppressing the international slave trade
    C) colonizing the interior of Africa
    D) establishing trading posts

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** After Great Britain abolished the slave trade in 1807, the text notes, "The British Royal Navy then played a significant role in trying to suppress the international slave trade."

### **Chapter 4: European Colonization of Africa**

51. The intense period of competition among European powers to annex territory in Africa from c. 1880-1914 is known as the
    A) Berlin Conference
    B) Scramble for Africa
    C) Triangular Trade
    D) Napoleonic Wars

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text defines the "Scramble for Africa" as "An intense period of competition among European powers... to claim and annex territory in Africa."

52. The Berlin Conference of 1884-85 was convened to
    A) grant independence to African nations
    B) regulate European colonization and avoid conflict among European powers
    C) abolish the slave trade in Africa
    D) promote trade between Africa and Europe

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states the conference was convened "to regulate European colonization and trade in Africa and to avoid direct conflict between European powers over African territory."

53. The principle of "Effective Occupation" established at the Berlin Conference meant that a European power had to
    A) gain the consent of the local people
    B) establish an administrative presence to claim a territory
    C) pay a fee to the German government
    D) have explored the territory for at least ten years

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "Effective Occupation" required a European power to "demonstrate 'effective occupation' (e.g., by establishing administrative presence, treaties with local rulers... or military control)."

54. The system of colonial administration favored by the British, which used traditional rulers, was known as
    A) Direct Rule
    B) Assimilation
    C) Association
    D) Indirect Rule

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Indirect Rule as being "Favored primarily by the British" and involved "Governing through existing traditional rulers and indigenous political institutions."

55. The French colonial policy that aimed to absorb colonial subjects into French culture and language was called
    A) Indirect Rule
    B) Segregation
    C) Assimilation
    D) Paternalism

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes Direct Rule as often aiming for "assimilation of colonial subjects into the colonizer's culture and language (especially French policy of 'assimilation')."

56. Yaa Asantewaa is a celebrated figure of resistance for leading the
    A) Mandinka resistance against the French
    B) Maji Maji Rebellion in German East Africa
    C) Asante uprising against the British
    D) Mahdist revolt in Sudan

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Yaa Asantewaa as the leader of "the Asante uprising (War of the Golden Stool) against British attempts to seize the Golden Stool."

57. One of the primary economic purposes of colonialism was to
    A) develop local industries in Africa
    B) extract raw materials for European industries
    C) promote balanced trade between Europe and Africa
    D) provide development aid to African colonies

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that a primary goal was the "Extraction of Raw Materials: Colonies were sources of raw materials... for European industries."

58. By 1914, the only two African countries that remained independent of European colonial rule were
    A) Egypt and Sudan
    B) Nigeria and Ghana
    C) Ethiopia and Liberia
    D) South Africa and Algeria

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text notes, "By 1914, almost the entire continent of Africa was under European colonial rule (except Ethiopia... and Liberia)."

59. The "White Man's Burden" was an ideological justification for colonialism based on
    A) economic necessity
    B) strategic advantage
    C) a belief in European racial and cultural superiority
    D) the need for new markets

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text links this ideology to the "Belief in European racial and cultural superiority (Social Darwinism, 'White Man's Burden')."

60. Samori Ture led a prolonged military resistance against
    A) the British in Nigeria
    B) the Belgians in the Congo
    C) the Germans in Namibia
    D) the French in West Africa

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Samori Ture as leading "a long and skillful military resistance against French colonial expansion."

61. What major technological advance greatly facilitated European conquest of the African interior?
    A) The printing press
    B) The compass
    C) The Maxim gun
    D) The telegraph

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists "improved weaponry (e.g., Maxim gun)" as a key technological advance that "facilitated European penetration and conquest of the interior."

62. Colonial boundaries in Africa were often drawn without regard for
    A) existing ethnic, linguistic, or political boundaries
    B) geographical features like rivers and mountains
    C) economic resources
    D) European convenience

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text states that the "division of Africa was done without regard for existing ethnic, linguistic, or political boundaries."

63. The British official most associated with articulating the theory of Indirect Rule was
    A) Otto von Bismarck
    B) King Leopold II
    C) Henry Morton Stanley
    D) Lord Lugard

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text credits the concept of Indirect Rule to "Lord Lugard," noting his role in its implementation.

64. A negative political legacy of colonialism in Africa is
    A) the introduction of universal suffrage
    B) the establishment of strong democratic traditions
    C) the creation of artificial boundaries leading to post-colonial conflicts
    D) the complete eradication of ethnic identities

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "These artificial boundaries became the borders of modern African nation-states... and have been a source of many post-colonial conflicts."

65. The Battle of Adwa in 1896 is significant because
    A) it marked the beginning of the Scramble for Africa
    B) Ethiopian forces defeated the Italians, preserving their independence
    C) the Asante were finally defeated by the British
    D) it was the first time a Maxim gun was used in Africa

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text highlights that Ethiopia "successfully resisted Italian colonization at Adwa in 1896."

66. Colonial economic policies often encouraged the production of
    A) food crops for local consumption
    B) cash crops for export
    C) manufactured goods for the local market
    D) heavy industrial machinery

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes, "Colonial policies often encouraged or forced the production of cash crops for export, sometimes at the expense of food crop production."

67. A major way colonial governments forced Africans into the wage economy was through
    A) high salaries
    B) the imposition of taxes payable in cash
    C) providing free housing
    D) offering educational grants

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains that colonial governments "imposed taxes (hut tax, poll tax) often payable in cash, forcing Africans to work in colonial enterprises... to earn money for taxes."

68. Railways built during the colonial era primarily served to
    A) link different African communities together
    B) promote integrated national development
    C) facilitate the extraction of resources and movement of troops
    D) encourage tourism

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text clarifies that infrastructure like railways "were built primarily to facilitate extraction of resources and movement of troops."

69. In practice, the distinction between direct and indirect rule was often
    A) very clear and strictly maintained
    B) blurred, with both systems aiming for control and exploitation
    C) decided by African rulers
    D) irrelevant to the lives of ordinary people

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states, "In practice, the distinction between direct and indirect rule was often blurred. Both systems aimed to maintain colonial control and facilitate economic exploitation."

70. "Warrant chiefs" were individuals appointed by the
    A) French to assist in direct rule
    B) British to act as rulers where no traditional chiefs existed
    C) Germans to collect taxes
    D) Belgians to oversee rubber collection

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains that under Indirect Rule, "Where centralized traditional structures did not exist, the British sometimes created them ('warrant chiefs')."

### **Chapter 5: Struggle for Independence in West Africa**

71. The political party formed by Kwame Nkrumah to demand immediate self-government for the Gold Coast was the
    A) United Gold Coast Convention (UGCC)
    B) Convention People's Party (CPP)
    C) National Council of Nigeria and the Cameroons (NCNC)
    D) Northern People's Congress (NPC)

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that Kwame Nkrumah formed the "Convention People's Party (CPP) (1949)" after splitting from the UGCC, with a more radical demand for "Self-Government Now!"

72. A major factor that fueled the rise of nationalism in West Africa after World War II was
    A) the economic prosperity brought by colonialism
    B) the experience of African soldiers who fought in the war
    C) the promise of continued European rule
    D) the success of the Transatlantic slave trade

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists among the contributing factors: "African soldiers fought for colonial powers, experiencing the world beyond their colonies and witnessing the 'vulnerability' of Europeans."

73. The intellectual and political movement advocating for the unity and self-determination of all people of African descent is known as
    A) Nationalism
    B) Colonialism
    C) Pan-Africanism
    D) Socialism

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines Pan-Africanism as "An intellectual and political movement advocating for the unity, solidarity, and self-determination of all people of African descent worldwide."

74. Nnamdi Azikiwe, Obafemi Awolowo, and Ahmadu Bello were key nationalist leaders in
    A) Ghana
    B) Sierra Leone
    C) Nigeria
    D) The Gambia

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists all three as key leaders in Nigeria: Azikiwe (NCNC), Awolowo (AG), and Bello (NPC).

75. The first sub-Saharan African country to gain independence from colonial rule was
    A) Nigeria in 1960
    B) Ghana in 1957
    C) Sierra Leone in 1961
    D) Guinea in 1958

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Ghana's independence in 1957 as a "landmark event" and notes it was the "first sub-Saharan African country to gain independence from colonial rule."

76. The strategy of strikes, boycotts, and civil disobedience advocated by Kwame Nkrumah was called
    A) Positive Action
    B) Passive Resistance
    C) Armed Struggle
    D) Gradualism

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that Nkrumah's CPP "used 'Positive Action' (strikes, boycotts, civil disobedience)."

77. In Nigeria, the Action Group (AG) party was primarily associated with the
    A) Northern Region and Hausa-Fulani people
    B) Eastern Region and Igbo people
    C) Western Region and Yoruba people
    D) Mid-West Region and Edo people

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Action Group (AG) as being "Led by Obafemi Awolowo. Primarily Yoruba-based, dominant in Western Nigeria."

78. The West African nation that gained immediate independence after voting "No" to the French Community in 1958 was
    A) Senegal
    B) Ivory Coast
    C) Guinea
    D) Mali

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states, "Under Sékou Touré, Guinea voted 'No' to remaining in Charles de Gaulle's proposed French Community... resulting in immediate and complete independence."

79. The United Gold Coast Convention (UGCC) was considered more conservative than the CPP because its slogan was
    A) "Self-Government Now!"
    B) "Independence or Death"
    C) "Africa for Africans"
    D) "self-government within the shortest possible time"

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text notes that the UGCC "Advocated for 'self-government within the shortest possible time'," which was a more gradualist approach compared to the CPP's radical slogan.

80. A major challenge faced by newly independent West African nations was
    A) a lack of diverse ethnic groups
    B) forging a sense of national identity within artificial colonial borders
    C) an over-developed industrial base
    D) too many experienced political leaders

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Nation-Building and National Unity" as a primary challenge, specifically "Forging a sense of national identity... among diverse ethnic, linguistic, and religious groups often arbitrarily brought together within colonial boundaries."

81. Herbert Macaulay is often called the "father of Nigerian nationalism" for his role in founding the
    A) Nigerian Youth Movement (NYM)
    B) Action Group (AG)
    C) National Council of Nigeria and the Cameroons (NCNC)
    D) Nigerian National Democratic Party (NNDP)

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Herbert Macaulay as the founder of the "Nigerian National Democratic Party (NNDP) (1923)" and calls him the "father of Nigerian nationalism."

82. The Rassemblement Démocratique Africain (RDA) was a major nationalist movement in
    A) British West Africa
    B) French West Africa
    C) Portuguese Guinea
    D) Belgian Congo

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text describes the RDA as "A major inter-territorial nationalist movement across French West Africa."

83. Obafemi Awolowo, as Premier of the Western Region of Nigeria, was known for advocating
    A) a unitary state and centralism
    B) for immediate unification with Ghana
    C) for federalism and implementing free primary education
    D) for a one-party system under the Action Group

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text highlights that Awolowo "Advocated for federalism, regional autonomy, and welfare policies (e.g., free primary education in Western Region)."

84. The concept of "neo-colonialism" refers to
    A) a second wave of direct European colonization
    B) the achievement of full economic independence by African nations
    C) indirect economic and political control exerted by former colonial powers after independence
    D) the creation of new colonies in Africa by other African states

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines neo-colonialism as "indirect economic/political control" that made new nations remain economically dependent on former colonial powers.

85. Sir Milton Margai was the first Prime Minister of independent
    A) Ghana
    B) Nigeria
    C) The Gambia
    D) Sierra Leone

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Sir Milton Margai as the leader of the SLPP and the "First Prime Minister of Sierra Leone."

86. Which international body provided a platform for colonized peoples to demand self-determination after WWII?
    A) The League of Nations
    B) The United Nations (UN)
    C) The Warsaw Pact
    D) The European Economic Community (EEC)

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that "The United Nations (UN): Provided a platform for colonized peoples to voice their demands for self-determination."

87. Léopold Sédar Senghor, a key nationalist leader in Senegal, was also a prominent figure in the
    A) Positive Action movement
    B) Negritude movement
    C) Pan-African Congress
    D) Anti-Apartheid movement

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text identifies Senghor as a "Poet, philosopher, political leader. Key figure in Negritude movement and RDA."

88. The 1945 Manchester Pan-African Congress was significant because it
    A) called for continued loyalty to colonial powers
    B) brought together African leaders who called for independence
    C) was the first to be held on African soil
    D) decided to abandon political action in favor of cultural festivals

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes that "Influential Pan-African Congresses (e.g., 1945 Manchester Congress) brought together African and diaspora leaders and called for independence."

89. Nigeria's first Prime Minister was
    A) Nnamdi Azikiwe
    B) Obafemi Awolowo
    C) Ahmadu Bello
    D) Abubakar Tafawa Balewa

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text states that after the 1959 elections, "Abubakar Tafawa Balewa (NPC) became Prime Minister."

90. In the early post-independence era, many West African economies remained dependent on
    A) a highly developed industrial sector
    B) the export of raw materials
    C) trade with other African nations
    D) financial aid from the Soviet Union

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** One of the key challenges listed is "Inherited colonial economies structured for export of raw materials and import of manufactured goods (dependent)."

### **Chapter 6: Political Developments Since Independence**

91. The Preventive Detention Act (1958) in Ghana under Nkrumah allowed for
    A) the release of all political prisoners
    B) detention without trial of persons deemed a security threat
    C) the establishment of multi-party democracy
    D) a referendum on the constitution

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states the Act "Allowed for detention without trial of individuals deemed a threat to state security. Used against political opponents."

92. The Nigerian Civil War was fought between 1967 and 1970 after the
    A) Western Region attempted to secede
    B) Northern Region declared independence
    C) Mid-West Region was invaded
    D) Eastern Region seceded and declared the Republic of Biafra

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text explains that the war began when "the Eastern Region, under Lt. Colonel Chukwuemeka Odumegwu Ojukwu, declared secession from Nigeria and proclaimed the independent Republic of Biafra."

93. The first military coup in Nigeria took place in
    A) October 1960
    B) January 1966
    C) July 1967
    D) January 1970

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text dates the first coup as "January 15, 1966," when young army officers overthrew the civilian government.

94. Kwame Nkrumah's strong advocacy for a "United States of Africa" is an example of his commitment to
    A) Nationalism
    B) Pan-Africanism
    C) Neo-colonialism
    D) Regionalism

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text highlights Nkrumah's Pan-Africanism, stating he "was a fervent advocate for African unity and the formation of a 'United States of Africa'."

95. The military regime that successfully handed over power to a civilian government in Nigeria in 1979 was led by
    A) Yakubu Gowon
    B) Murtala Muhammed / Olusegun Obasanjo
    C) Ibrahim Babangida
    D) Sani Abacha

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes that after Murtala's assassination, "Obasanjo continued plan" for a return to civilian rule, which culminated in the Second Republic in 1979.

96. The slogan "No Victor, No Vanquished" was associated with Yakubu Gowon's policy of
    A) starting the Nigerian Civil War
    B) reconciliation after the Nigerian Civil War
    C) creating more states in Nigeria
    D) celebrating the military's victory over Biafra

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that at the end of the war, "Gowon declared 'No Victor, No Vanquished' and initiated a policy of reconciliation, reabsorption, and reconstruction."

97. ECOMOG, the military intervention force of ECOWAS, was notably deployed in the 1990s to restore peace in
    A) Nigeria and Ghana
    B) Liberia and Sierra Leone
    C) South Africa and Namibia
    D) Mali and Niger

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text gives examples of ECOMOG's deployments, including "Liberia, Sierra Leone in 1990s."

98. The annulment of the June 12, 1993 presidential election in Nigeria occurred under the military regime of
    A) Sani Abacha
    B) Muhammadu Buhari
    C) Ibrahim Babangida
    D) Abdulsalami Abubakar

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that the "Ibrahim Babangida regime... Annulled the June 12, 1993 presidential election."

99. The African Union (AU) policy that allows for intervention in member states in grave circumstances is known as the policy of
    A) Non-Interference
    B) Non-Indifference
    C) Non-Alignment
    D) Effective Occupation

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains the "Policy of Non-Indifference: Shift from OAU's non-interference principle, allowing AU to intervene in member states in grave circumstances."

100. Kwame Nkrumah was overthrown in a military coup in 1966 while he was
    A) addressing the United Nations
    B) on a state visit to China and Vietnam
    C) attending an OAU summit in Addis Ababa
    D) sleeping in his presidential palace

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states, "Nkrumah was overthrown in a military coup in February 1966 while he was on a state visit to China and Vietnam."

101. A major political characteristic of Nigeria's First Republic was
    A) a highly centralized unitary government
    B) the dominance of a single national political party
    C) intense ethnic regionalism and rivalry
    D) a long period of peace and stability

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Ethnic Regionalism and Rivalry: Intense competition for power and resources between the major ethnic groups and regions" as a key challenge of the First Republic.

102. The military regime of Sani Abacha in Nigeria (1993-1998) was known for being
    A) highly democratic and open
    B) economically prosperous and stable
    C) highly authoritarian and repressive
    D) committed to a swift return to civilian rule

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Sani Abacha regime as "Highly authoritarian and repressive. Human rights abuses... International isolation."

103. The primary mandate of the Peace and Security Council (PSC) of the African Union is to
    A) promote economic integration
    B) prevent, manage, and resolve conflicts
    C) observe elections in member states
    D) coordinate cultural festivals

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the PSC as the "Main AU organ for prevention, management, and resolution of conflicts."

104. A common justification cited by military officers for staging coups in West Africa was
    A) the need to uphold the constitution
    B) a desire for lower military budgets
    C) corruption and mismanagement by civilian governments
    D) pressure from former colonial powers

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that justifications for coups "Often cited corruption, mismanagement, political instability, ethnic divisions under civilian governments."

105. Nigeria's return to civilian democratic rule in 1999, known as the Fourth Republic, began with the presidency of
    A) Shehu Shagari
    B) Olusegun Obasanjo
    C) M.K.O. Abiola
    D) Abdulsalami Abubakar

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes the Fourth Republic began in 1999 "with Olusegun Obasanjo (PDP) as President."

106. The immediate trigger for the July 1966 counter-coup in Nigeria was
    A) the secession of Biafra
    B) the assassination of Murtala Muhammed
    C) a controversial federal election
    D) a reaction to the first coup and Ironsi's unification decree

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text explains the counter-coup was "partly in reaction to perceived Igbo dominance in first coup and Ironsi's unification decree (which abolished federalism)."

107. The Volta River Project, featuring the Akosombo Dam, was a major economic development initiative in
    A) Nigeria under Balewa
    B) Ghana under Nkrumah
    C) Sierra Leone under Margai
    D) Guinea under Touré

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions the "Volta River Project for hydroelectric power and Akosombo Dam" as an example of Nkrumah's focus on economic development in Ghana.

108. The ECOWAS Protocol on Democracy and Good Governance condemns
    A) free market economies
    B) unconstitutional changes of government
    C) Pan-Africanism
    D) the use of peacekeeping forces

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text says the protocol "Condemns unconstitutional changes of government (e.g., coups)."

109. Which military leader initiated the plan for a return to civilian rule in Nigeria in the mid-1970s before his assassination?
    A) Yakubu Gowon
    B) Johnson Aguiyi-Ironsi
    C) Murtala Muhammed
    D) Sani Abacha

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that "Murtala Muhammed... initiated reforms, anti-corruption drive, plan for return to civilian rule (assassinated 1976)."

110. One of the main reasons for public discontent leading to the 1966 coup against Nkrumah was
    A) his refusal to support other African liberation movements
    B) growing authoritarianism and economic difficulties
    C) his close alliance with the United States
    D) the failure to build the Akosombo Dam

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists factors contributing to the coup, including "Growing authoritarianism, economic problems, suppression of opposition."

### **Chapter 7: Traditional Societies, Religion, and Culture**

111. A kinship system that traces descent and inheritance through the male line is described as
    A) matrilineal
    B) bilateral
    C) patrilineal
    D) extended

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines Patrilineal Descent as "Tracing descent and inheritance through the male line (father's side)."

112. Social organizations based on age, where individuals pass through stages like warriorhood and elderhood together, are known as
    A) clans
    B) lineages
    C) chieftaincy institutions
    D) age grades and age sets

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text explains that Age Grades and Age Sets are "Social organizations based on age, where individuals of similar age are grouped together and pass through different stages of life and responsibility collectively."

113. In many traditional African religions, deceased ancestors are venerated because they are believed to
    A) be evil spirits that must be appeased
    B) have become all-powerful gods
    C) be intermediaries between the living and the spirit world
    D) have no further connection to their living descendants

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "Ancestors are seen as intermediaries between the living and the Supreme Being or other spirits."

114. The arrival of Islam in West Africa was primarily facilitated by
    A) European missionaries
    B) Trans-Saharan trade routes
    C) sea voyages from the Middle East
    D) the conquest by the Byzantine Empire

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that Islam was "Introduced primarily from North Africa through the Trans-Saharan trade routes from around the 8th-9th centuries onwards."

115. Kente cloth is a famous textile art form associated with the people of
    A) Nigeria
    B) Mali
    C) Ghana
    D) Senegal

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Kente cloth (Ghana - Asante and Ewe)" as a prime example of a rich textile tradition.

116. A form of oral literature that consists of long narrative poems celebrating heroic deeds is
    A) proverbs
    B) riddles
    C) epic poetry
    D) folktales

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines Epic Poetry as "Long narrative poems celebrating heroic deeds and historical events (e.g., Epic of Sundiata)."

117. The major spread of Christianity in West Africa occurred in the 19th and 20th centuries, closely associated with
    A) the Trans-Saharan trade
    B) the activities of Islamic scholars
    C) European colonialism and missionary societies
    D) the establishment of the Oyo Empire

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that the major spread of Christianity was "Associated with European colonialism and the activities of Christian missionary societies (Catholic and Protestant)."

118. In indigenous Yoruba religion, deities like Sango and Ogun are known as
    A) Abosom
    B) Loa
    C) Orishas
    D) Jinn

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text gives "Orishas in Yoruba religion (e.g., Sango, Ogun, Yemoja)" as an example of lesser divinities.

119. A key function of chieftaincy institutions in traditional societies was
    A) promoting trade with Europe
    B) settling disputes according to customary law
    C) establishing written constitutions
    D) encouraging the abandonment of traditional culture

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Among the roles of traditional rulers, the text lists "Judicial functions (settling disputes according to customary law)."

120. The blending of different religious beliefs and practices, such as Islam or Christianity with indigenous traditions, is known as
    A) orthodoxy
    B) fundamentalism
    C) syncretism
    D) atheism

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text frequently uses the term syncretism to describe how "Islam blended with existing indigenous beliefs and practices" and how African Independent Churches "blended Christian beliefs with African cultural expressions."

121. What is the name of the West African trickster figure, often depicted as a spider, who is prominent in folktales?
    A) Sundiata
    B) Ananse
    C) Shaka
    D) Bayajidda

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions "trickster figures (e.g., Ananse the spider)" as a feature of folktales.

122. In many traditional West African societies, women often dominated
    A) long-distance trade
    B) political leadership roles
    C) local market trade
    D) warfare and hunting

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text notes that women were often responsible for "local market trade (women often dominated local markets)."

123. The transfer of wealth from the groom's family to the bride's family in many traditional marriage systems is known as
    A) dowry
    B) bride-price or bride-wealth
    C) alimony
    D) inheritance

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states that marriage "Often involved bride-price or bride-wealth (transfer of goods or wealth from groom's family to bride's family)."

124. The practice of pouring drinks as an offering to ancestors or spirits is called
    A) divination
    B) sacrifice
    C) libation
    D) initiation

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text mentions that ancestors are honored through rituals including "prayers, libations, and offerings."

125. Christian missionaries contributed to the study of African languages primarily by
    A) discouraging their use in favor of European languages
    B) translating the Bible into local languages
    C) recording oral epics and poems
    D) creating dictionaries for trade purposes

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes that "Missionaries often translated the Bible into local languages, contributing to the study and standardization of these languages."

126. The instrument known as the Kora is a type of
    A) drum
    B) xylophone
    C) harp-lute
    D) flute

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists the "Kora (harp-lute)" as an example of a chordophone (stringed instrument).

127. The development of African Independent Churches (AICs) was partly a response to
    A) the spread of Islam
    B) European missionary control and a desire for cultural expression
    C) a lack of interest in religion among Africans
    D) orders from colonial governments

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text explains that AICs blended Christian beliefs with African cultural expressions, "sometimes as a form of resistance to European missionary control."

128. In many traditional African religions, the Supreme Being is often seen as
    A) directly involved in everyday human affairs
    B) a malevolent entity to be feared
    C) remote and not directly worshipped
    D) one of many equal gods

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Supreme Being as "often seen as remote or distant from everyday human affairs" and "Usually not directly worshipped with elaborate rituals."

129. The Epic of Sundiata is an oral tradition that tells the story of the founding of the
    A) Oyo Empire
    B) Benin Kingdom
    C) Mali Empire
    D) Sokoto Caliphate

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The Epic of Sundiata (though the specific empire is not mentioned in the provided snippet for Chapter 7, it's a well-known fact associated with the example given) is the foundational epic of the Mali Empire. The text uses it as a key example of epic poetry.

130. A key characteristic of traditional African music is the use of
    A) simple, single rhythms
    B) written musical scores
    C) polyrhythms and call-and-response patterns
    D) string orchestras

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text highlights characteristics of African music as "Often highly rhythmic and polyrhythmic" and notes that "Call-and-response patterns are common."

### **Chapter 8: Urbanization, Education, and Social Change**

131. A major driver of urbanization in post-colonial West Africa has been
    A) government policies forcing people into cities
    B) rural-urban migration in search of economic opportunities
    C) the decline of all agricultural activities
    D) the pleasant living conditions in all urban areas

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text identifies that post-colonial urbanization accelerated due to "Rural-Urban Migration: Large numbers of people moving from rural areas to towns and cities in search of better economic opportunities."

132. The primary aim of colonial education policies was often to
    A) provide university education for all Africans
    B) preserve and promote indigenous cultures
    C) produce low-level administrators and clerks for the colonial system
    D) create a large class of industrial engineers

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that the aims of colonial education were "Often limited to producing a small cadre of low-level administrators, clerks, and technicians needed for the colonial administration."

133. A common feature of colonial cities like Lagos and Accra was
    A) the full integration of European and African populations
    B) the absence of any infrastructure
    C) the existence of segregated residential areas
    D) the dominance of pre-colonial architecture

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the "Creation of Segregated Urban Spaces" with distinct European residential areas separate from often overcrowded African townships.

134. After independence, a key goal of post-colonial education reforms was to
    A) reduce the number of schools
    B) Africanize curricula to make them more locally relevant
    C) focus exclusively on religious education
    D) abolish higher education

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Africanizing curricula to make them more relevant to local culture, history, and needs" as a primary goal of post-colonial education reforms.

135. In many traditional West African societies, gender roles were often
    A) identical for men and women
    B) fixed and unchangeable
    C) complementary, with a division of labor
    D) determined by colonial officials

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text notes that "Gender roles were often complementary, with both men and women contributing to household and community well-being."

136. A significant social change in post-colonial West Africa has been the shift towards
    A) larger extended families in urban areas
    B) more nuclear families, especially in urban areas
    C) the complete disappearance of the family unit
    D) a return to purely traditional family structures

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text notes a "Shift from predominantly extended family systems towards more nuclear families, especially in urban areas."

137. The term 'brain drain' refers to the
    A) loss of cultural knowledge
    B) emigration of educated professionals from their home countries
    C) decline in educational standards
    D) impact of diseases on cognitive function

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Under the challenges of post-colonial education, the text lists "Brain drain (emigration of educated professionals)."

138. Colonialism often impacted gender roles by
    A) giving women more political power than men
    B) favoring men for wage labor and Western education
    C) creating perfect equality between the sexes
    D) ignoring gender altogether

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states, "Men were often favored for colonial wage labor, cash crop production, and Western education, while women's roles were often confined to subsistence farming or domestic sphere."

139. A major challenge associated with rapid and unplanned urbanization in West Africa is
    A) a surplus of housing and services
    B) a strain on urban infrastructure and services
    C) under-population of cities
    D) a decline in cultural activities

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Strain on urban infrastructure and services (water, sanitation, electricity, transport, housing, waste management)" as a key characteristic of post-colonial urban growth.

140. West African women's movements in the post-colonial era have typically campaigned for
    A) a return to strict traditional roles
    B) greater political representation and gender equality
    C) the exclusion of men from public life
    D) the abolition of all forms of trade

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text indicates that women's movements campaign for "Gender equality and women's rights" and "Greater political representation."

141. The establishment of Government Reservation Areas (GRAs) in colonial cities is an example of
    A) urban planning for the poor
    B) promotion of cultural integration
    C) development of industrial zones
    D) residential segregation based on race

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies GRAs as "distinct European residential areas with better amenities, separate from African townships," which is a clear example of segregation.

142. Universal Primary Education (UPE) was a common goal of
    A) pre-colonial education systems
    B) colonial education policies
    C) post-colonial education reforms
    D) missionary societies in the 19th century

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that after independence, "Universal Primary Education - UPE became a common goal" for new African nations.

143. Timbuktu, Gao, and Kano are examples of
    A) cities that only developed during the colonial era
    B) significant pre-colonial urban centers
    C) small fishing villages
    D) European-style capitals

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions these cities as examples of places where "West Africa had a history of urbanism before colonialism."

144. One of the social impacts of major health crises like COVID-19 is the
    A) strengthening of all traditional practices
    B) complete stop of all economic activity
    C) disruption of education due to school closures
    D) permanent improvement of all health systems

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly lists "Education: Widespread school closures" and the resulting challenges as a key social impact of the COVID-19 pandemic.

145. "Pull" factors for rural-urban migration include
    A) famine and poverty in rural areas
    B) the search for better education and jobs in cities
    C) lack of land in rural areas
    D) conflict in the countryside

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text indicates people move to cities "in search of better economic opportunities (jobs), education, healthcare, and modern amenities," which are pull factors that attract them to urban areas.

### **Chapter 12: Contemporary African History and the Global 21st Century**

146. The rapid and widespread adoption of which technology has been particularly transformative in 21st-century Africa?
    A) Landline telephones
    B) Mainframe computers
    C) Mobile phones
    D) Telegraphs

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text highlights that the "Rapid and widespread adoption of mobile phones across Africa has been transformative," leading to innovations like mobile banking.

147. The #EndSARS movement in Nigeria (2020) was a youth-led protest primarily against
    A) climate change
    B) economic globalization
    C) police brutality
    D) election results

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text describes the #EndSARS movement as "Large-scale, youth-led protests initially against police brutality by the Special Anti-Robbery Squad (SARS)."

148. The Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) are a set of 17 global goals adopted by the
    A) African Union
    B) United Nations
    C) World Bank
    D) G7 countries

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text states the SDGs are "a set of 17 global goals adopted by all UN Member States in 2015."

149. A major challenge for Africa regarding climate change is that
    A) the continent contributes the most to global emissions
    B) the continent is one of the most vulnerable to its impacts
    C) climate change is not affecting Africa
    D) African governments have refused to acknowledge the problem

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text clearly states, "Africa is one of the most vulnerable continents to the impacts of climate change, despite contributing the least to historical global greenhouse gas emissions."

150. The #BringBackOurGirls movement was an advocacy campaign demanding
    A) the end of police brutality in Nigeria
    B) the rescue of schoolgirls abducted by Boko Haram
    C) political reforms in Senegal
    D) the resignation of the president of Burkina Faso

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the #BringBackOurGirls movement as an "Advocacy campaign demanding the rescue of schoolgirls abducted by Boko Haram in Chibok."

151. The "digital divide" in Africa refers to
    A) the gap between different mobile phone brands
    B) disparities in access to the internet and digital technology
    C) the difference between digital and analog clocks
    D) competition between technology companies

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text mentions "significant disparities (digital divide) within and between countries (urban vs. rural, income levels)" regarding internet access.

152. Africa CDC is the continent's institution for
    A) coordinating economic policy
    B) promoting cultural heritage
    C) disease control and prevention
    D) settling political disputes

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text mentions "Africa CDC (Centres for Disease Control and Prevention)" played a coordinating role during the COVID-19 pandemic.

153. A key characteristic of 21st-century youth movements like #EndSARS is the extensive use of
    A) printed newspapers
    B) radio broadcasts
    C) town criers
    D) social media for mobilization

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text notes that these movements are "often leveraging technology and social media to mobilize" and that #EndSARS "Mobilized extensively through social media."

154. A major focus of climate change adaptation measures in Africa is
    A) building more coal-fired power plants
    B) increasing historical greenhouse gas emissions
    C) building resilience through measures like drought-resistant crops
    D) denying the existence of climate change

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text states that "Adaptation Measures: Focus on building resilience to climate impacts (e.g., drought-resistant crops, water harvesting...)."

155. "Vaccine nationalism" during the COVID-19 pandemic refers to
    A) the refusal of African nations to accept vaccines
    B) the development of traditional vaccines in Africa
    C) global competition where wealthier nations secured vaccine supplies for themselves first
    D) the mandatory vaccination of all citizens in a country

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explains that due to "global competition for vaccines... African countries often facing delays and shortages ('vaccine nationalism' by wealthier nations)."

156. The growth of tech hubs and innovation ecosystems in cities like Lagos, Nairobi, and Accra is part of Africa's
    A) digital revolution
    B) green revolution
    C) industrial revolution
    D) political revolution

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text mentions the "Growth of tech hubs and innovation ecosystems in some African cities" as a key part of the "Digital Revolutions in Africa."

157. The "Y'en a Marre" ("Fed Up") movement played a role in promoting democratic transition in
    A) Nigeria
    B) Ghana
    C) Burkina Faso
    D) Senegal

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text identifies "Y'en a Marre" as a "Youth-led civil society movement" in "Senegal" that protested against President Wade.

158. SDG 1 aims to
    A) ensure quality education
    B) achieve gender equality
    C) end poverty in all its forms
    D) provide clean water and sanitation

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists the SDGs and their aims, noting Goal 1 is to address "poverty."

159. An example of a climate change mitigation measure in Africa would be
    A) building sea walls to protect against rising sea levels
    B) developing early warning systems for droughts
    C) investing in solar and wind energy
    D) creating new irrigation systems

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Investing in renewable energy is a mitigation measure aimed at reducing emissions, whereas the other options are adaptation measures. The text mentions "investing in renewable energy - solar, wind, geothermal" as a mitigation strategy.

160. What demographic feature makes youth activism particularly significant in 21st-century Africa?
    A) Africa has the world's oldest population
    B) Africa has the world's youngest population
    C) The youth population is declining rapidly
    D) Most young people live in rural areas

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text provides the "Demographic Context: Africa has the world's youngest population (large youth bulge)," which is a key driver of youth movements.

### **Chapter 13: Approaches to Historical Research (Applied)**

161. An oral history interview is considered a
    A) secondary source
    B) primary source
    C) tertiary source
    D) historiographical source

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Oral history collects firsthand recollections, which falls under the definition of a primary source. The text describes it as collecting information from individuals with "personal experiences."

162. The first step in conducting an oral history interview is
    A) transcribing the interview
    B) publishing the results
    C) conducting thorough research and preparation
    D) analyzing the data

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Preparation and Research" as the first key technique, involving researching the topic and developing questions before the interview.

163. A key ethical consideration in oral history is obtaining
    A) a financial payment for the interviewee
    B) a signed confession
    C) informed consent from the interviewee
    D) the approval of the government

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text strongly emphasizes "Informed Consent: Interviewees must fully understand the purpose of the interview... and their rights."

164. The official records of a country's government are typically held in its
    A) National Museum
    B) National Library
    C) National Archives
    D) Presidential Palace

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines National Archives as holding the "Official records of a country's government."

165. Catalogs, indexes, and inventories that describe an archive's collections are known as
    A) finding aids
    B) research notes
    C) primary sources
    D) bibliographies

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "Finding Aids: Catalogs, indexes, inventories, guides that describe the archive's collections" are used to help locate records.

166. A historical essay must be guided by a clear and arguable
    A) bibliography
    B) quotation
    C) research question
    D) thesis statement

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** The text emphasizes that a historical essay "presents a thesis (an interpretation or claim about a historical topic)" and that "The thesis guides your essay."

167. The process of converting a recorded interview into a written text is called
    A) analysis
    B) summary
    C) transcription
    D) synthesis

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text defines Transcription as "The process of converting the audio or video recording of an interview into a written text."

168. Using someone else's words or ideas without proper attribution is a serious academic offense known as
    A) corroboration
    B) synthesis
    C) plagiarism
    D) contextualization

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly warns against "Plagiarism (using someone else's words or ideas without proper attribution)."

169. When evaluating a written primary source, a historian considers the author's purpose, position, and potential biases. This is a form of
    A) external criticism
    B) internal criticism
    C) chronology
    D) archival management

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text lists considering the "Author/Creator: Who created it? What was their position, background, expertise, potential biases, purpose..." as part of Internal Criticism (Reliability/Credibility).

170. Which of the following is a key difference between oral history and oral tradition?
    A) Oral history is written, while oral tradition is spoken
    B) Oral history focuses on an individual's own lifetime experiences, while oral tradition is passed down through generations
    C) Oral tradition is more reliable than oral history
    D) Oral history is a secondary source, while oral tradition is a primary source

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text distinguishes between the two, noting that "oral history usually focuses on an individual's own lifetime or direct recollections," whereas oral tradition is "passed down through generations."

171. When working in an archive, what is a crucial rule for handling documents?
    A) Use a pen for taking notes
    B) Handle documents with extreme care, often using only a pencil
    C) Fold documents in new ways to preserve them
    D) Eat and drink while working to save time

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text gives practical advice for archival research, including "Handle documents with extreme care (clean hands, pencils only, no food/drink...)."

172. The conclusion of a historical essay should
    A) introduce new evidence and arguments
    B) restate the thesis and summarize the main points
    C) be the longest part of the essay
    D) end with a question for the reader

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text advises that the conclusion should "Restate your thesis in different words. Summarize your main supporting arguments." and "Avoid introducing new evidence."

173. Digital repositories are
    A) physical buildings for storing old computers
    B) online collections of digitized historical materials
    C) a type of secondary source only
    D) systems for destroying old documents

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text describes "Digital Repositories and Online Archives" as places where "archival materials and historical documents are being digitized and made available online."

174. The Chicago Manual of Style is mentioned as a common
    A) method for conducting interviews
    B) type of archival finding aid
    C) citation style used in history writing
    D) school of historiography

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Chicago Manual of Style (notes-bibliography or author-date), Turabian" as "Common styles in history" for citations.

175. The first step in evaluating a secondary source, such as a book by a historian, is to
    A) check the price of the book
    B) assess the author's credentials, expertise, and potential biases
    C) count the number of pages
    D) assume everything in it is factual and objective

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The text advises that when evaluating secondary sources, one should consider the "Author's credentials, expertise, and potential biases or theoretical perspective."

### **Combined Review Questions (Chapters 1-13)**

176. The system of checks and balances in the Oyo Empire, involving the Oyo Mesi and the Ogboni society, is an example of
    A) a traditional political system
    B) a colonial administrative policy
    C) a nationalist party structure
    D) a post-independence military government

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** This political structure is described in Chapter 2 as a key feature of the pre-colonial Oyo Empire's governance.

177. The building of railways by colonial powers and the modern expansion of fiber optic cables in Africa both primarily relate to the theme of
    A) political resistance
    B) religious change
    C) infrastructure development
    D) cultural festivals

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Both are examples of infrastructure. Chapter 4 discusses colonial infrastructure, and Chapter 12 discusses modern digital infrastructure.

178. The "Scramble for Africa" and the "Triangular Trade" were both historical processes driven primarily by
    A) African desires for unity
    B) European economic and political interests
    C) United Nations resolutions
    D) the spread of Islamic jihads

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 explains the European drivers of the slave trade, and Chapter 4 explains the European motives for colonization, both centering on economic gain and political power.

179. A historian studying the #EndSARS movement would find Twitter posts from 2020 to be a valuable
    A) secondary source
    B) oral tradition
    C) primary source
    D) historiographical review

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** As per the definition in Chapter 1, these posts are original records created by participants during the event, making them primary sources. Chapter 12 discusses the movement's use of social media.

180. Which of the following pairs a historical leader with the correct resistance or political movement?
    A) Samori Ture - The Convention People's Party
    B) Yaa Asantewaa - The #EndSARS Movement
    C) Kwame Nkrumah - The Asante Uprising
    D) Nnamdi Azikiwe - The National Council of Nigeria and the Cameroons (NCNC)

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 correctly identifies Azikiwe as a leader of the NCNC. The other pairings are incorrect.

181. A study focusing on the role of the Orishas in Yoruba belief and the impact of the Sokoto Caliphate on Hausaland would fall under which two branches of history?
    A) Political and Economic
    B) Social and Military
    C) Cultural and Political
    D) Economic and Diplomatic

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The study of Orishas is a part of Cultural History (or religious history, a sub-branch). The Sokoto Caliphate was a major political entity, so its study is Political History.

182. The principle of "Effective Occupation" from the Berlin Conference can be contrasted with the AU's modern policy of
    A) Non-Indifference
    B) Pan-Africanism
    C) condemning unconstitutional changes of government
    D) free trade

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** "Effective Occupation" was a rule for legitimizing colonial seizure of power. The AU's condemnation of unconstitutional changes of government is a modern principle for legitimizing *governments*, representing a stark contrast in political philosophy.

183. Which event occurred first chronologically?
    A) The Nigerian Civil War
    B) The establishment of the Sokoto Caliphate
    C) The Berlin Conference
    D) The independence of Ghana

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The Sokoto Caliphate was established in the early 19th century. The Berlin Conference was in 1884-85. Ghana's independence was in 1957. The Nigerian Civil War was 1967-70.

184. A historian using Lord Lugard's book "The Dual Mandate in British Tropical Africa" to understand colonial policy is using a
    A) material artifact
    B) secondary source created centuries later
    C) primary source written by a key historical actor
    D) fictional narrative

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Lugard was a key participant and architect of the policy he is writing about. Therefore, his book serves as a primary source for understanding his own ideas and the rationale behind the policy.

185. The historical method of contextualization would be most crucial for understanding
    A) why the Benin Bronzes are artistically significant
    B) the exact date of the first military coup in Nigeria
    C) why Sékou Touré's "No" vote in 1958 was a radical and risky decision
    D) the chemical composition of Nok terracotta

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Understanding the context of the Cold War, French colonial policy (assimilation/community), and the dependent position of colonies is essential to grasp the full significance and risk of Guinea's decision to opt for immediate independence.

186. The legacy of slavery and the legacy of colonialism both contributed to
    A) Africa's rapid and balanced industrialization
    B) the economic underdevelopment and dependency of many African nations
    C) the establishment of strong, unified nations with no ethnic tensions
    D) a long history of peaceful relations with Europe

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Both Chapters 3 and 4 discuss how these processes extracted resources (human and material) and created dependent economic structures, contributing to long-term underdevelopment.

187. The spread of Islam via the Trans-Saharan trade and the spread of Christianity via European missionaries are both examples of
    A) cultural diffusion
    B) political revolution
    C) economic exploitation
    D) military conquest

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Both are processes where cultural elements (in this case, major religions) spread from one society to another, a concept known as cultural diffusion. This is detailed in Chapter 7.

188. Which of the following represents a shift from a pre-colonial to a post-colonial challenge?
    A) From inter-state warfare for captives to managing ethnic tensions within a modern state
    B) From trading salt for gold to trading oil for manufactured goods
    C) From using oral tradition to preserve history to using digital archives
    D) All of the above

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** All three options represent a correct and significant shift. A) represents a political shift from pre-colonial conflict to post-colonial nation-state issues. B) represents a major economic shift. C) represents a shift in historical methodology and technology.
