189. The political organization of the Hausa city-states before the Fulani jihad was most similar to that of
    A) Ancient Egypt
    B) the centralized Benin Kingdom
    C) the Greek city-states
    D) the unified Songhai Empire

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The Hausa states were described as independent "city-states," each with its own ruler (Sarki). This is politically analogous to the famous Greek city-states like Athens and Sparta, which were also independent political entities.

190. A historian arguing that the primary motive for the Scramble for Africa was the search for markets and raw materials is using which historiographical lens?
    A) Feminist
    B) Afrocentric
    C) Economic (similar to Marxist focus on material factors)
    D) Postmodern

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** This argument places primary importance on economic factors, which is a hallmark of economic history and is central to Marxist historiography.

191. The development of syncretic religions in the African Diaspora and the creation of African Independent Churches in Africa are both examples of
    A) complete rejection of European beliefs
    B) blending foreign religious ideas with African cultural expressions
    C) a movement towards atheism
    D) government-mandated religious policies

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 discusses syncretism in the diaspora, and Chapter 7 discusses it in the context of AICs in Africa. Both involve mixing and adapting beliefs.

192. Which two leaders represent contrasting approaches to achieving independence from colonial rule?
    A) J.B. Danquah (gradualist) and Kwame Nkrumah (immediatist)
    B) Nnamdi Azikiwe and Obafemi Awolowo (both Nigerian nationalists)
    C) Samori Ture and Yaa Asantewaa (both primary military resisters)
    D) Lord Lugard and Otto von Bismarck (both European colonial figures)

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 contrasts the UGCC's (led by figures like Danquah) call for "self-government in the shortest possible time" with the CPP's (led by Nkrumah) radical demand for "Self-Government Now!"

193. The function of age-grades in traditional societies can be compared to the function of what modern institution?
    A) A political party
    B) A national education and social security system
    C) A stock exchange
    D) A multinational corporation

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 7 describes age-grades as being responsible for education, military service, communal labor, and social discipline, which are functions now largely handled by national institutions like schools, armies, and social welfare programs.

194. A major continuity from the colonial era to the post-colonial era in many African economies is
    A) the dominance of subsistence agriculture
    B) a heavy reliance on the export of primary commodities
    C) the focus on trade with other African countries
    D) the absence of foreign investment

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 explains how colonial economies were built on raw material extraction, and Chapter 5 notes that newly independent nations inherited and often continued these dependent economic structures.

195. The historical study of the Benin Kingdom's art, the political structure of the Oyo Empire, and the religious beliefs of the Akan people would require a historian to primarily use which approach?
    A) Relying solely on European written records
    B) A combination of archaeology, oral traditions, and art historical analysis
    C) Focusing only on post-colonial documents
    D) Conducting surveys of modern populations

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Understanding these pre-literate or partially literate societies requires a multi-disciplinary approach. Archaeology provides material culture (like the bronzes), oral traditions preserve history and beliefs, and art history analyzes the artifacts.

196. The issue of "vaccine equity" in the 21st century mirrors what historical issue from the 19th century?
    A) The unequal distribution of military technology during the Scramble for Africa
    B) The debate over direct vs. indirect rule
    C) The competition between nationalist parties
    D) The rise of Pan-Africanism

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Both situations highlight a global power imbalance where technologically advanced nations hold a life-or-death advantage over less developed ones, whether through superior weaponry (Maxim gun) or preferential access to life-saving medicine (vaccines).

197. A key difference between the Trans-Saharan and Transatlantic slave trades was that
    A) the Transatlantic trade was much shorter in duration
    B) the Trans-Saharan trade did not involve African intermediaries
    C) the Transatlantic trade was primarily for plantation chattel slavery in the Americas
    D) the Trans-Saharan trade involved more people

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** While both were brutal, Chapter 3 highlights that the Transatlantic trade was driven specifically by the demand for labor on large-scale plantations in the Americas, leading to a system of chattel slavery that was distinct in scale and nature from the uses of slaves in the Trans-Saharan system (domestic service, soldiers, etc.).

198. The political figure Sékou Touré is to Guinea as ________ is to Ghana.
    A) Nnamdi Azikiwe
    B) Jomo Kenyatta
    C) Kwame Nkrumah
    D) Léopold Senghor

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Sékou Touré was the pivotal leader who led Guinea to independence. Kwame Nkrumah played the same paramount role in leading Ghana to independence.

199. The use of a "talking drum" to convey messages in traditional West Africa is an example of
    A) written record
    B) a form of indigenous communication technology
    C) a European introduction
    D) religious ritual only

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The talking drum, mentioned in Chapter 7's section on music, is a sophisticated instrument used for communication, representing an indigenous form of technology long before modern methods.

200. The creation of the Sokoto Caliphate and the establishment of colonial Nigeria both represent
    A) processes of political unification and state-building on a large scale
    B) movements led by indigenous people to expel foreigners
    C) examples of peaceful political transitions
    D) failures to establish any form of government

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The Sokoto Caliphate (Chapter 2) unified the Hausa states under one Islamic empire. Colonial Nigeria (Chapter 4) forcibly unified diverse peoples into a single political entity. Both were large-scale state-building projects, albeit with vastly different origins and goals.

---
### **Additional Thematic and Comparative Questions**

201. Which of the following civilizations was NOT located in the geographical area of modern-day Nigeria?
    A) Nok
    B) Kanem-Bornu
    C) Ife
    D) Benin

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** While the Kanem-Bornu Empire extended into parts of modern Nigeria, its center was around Lake Chad, encompassing present-day Chad, Niger, and Cameroon as well. The other three (Nok, Ife, Benin) are all centrally located within Nigeria's historical geography.

202. The concept of "divine kingship" was a central feature of the political authority in
    A) Ancient Egypt and the Oyo Empire
    B) The Nok Civilization and the Hausa States
    C) Colonial Nigeria and Post-colonial Ghana
    D) The Asante Kingdom and the French colonies

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The text describes the Pharaoh of Egypt as a "divine or semi-divine king" and the Alaafin of Oyo as a "divine ruler." This was a common feature of many powerful pre-colonial monarchies.

203. A historian analyzing the impact of the introduction of firearms in both the slave trade and the colonial conquest would be studying the role of ________ in history.
    A) diplomacy
    B) technology
    C) religion
    D) economics

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Firearms (like the Maxim gun) are a form of military technology. Their introduction fueled the slave trade by empowering raiding states and later gave Europeans a decisive advantage in the colonial conquest.

204. The political instability of Nigeria's First Republic and the later authoritarianism of Nkrumah's Ghana both demonstrate
    A) the success of European-style democracy in Africa
    B) the severe challenges of post-independence governance and nation-building
    C) the complete absence of economic development after independence
    D) the desire of all Africans for military rule

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Both cases, detailed in Chapter 6, highlight different facets of the immense difficulties new nations faced, including managing ethnic rivalry (Nigeria) and balancing development with democracy (Ghana).

205. The preservation of history through oral traditions like the Epic of Sundiata is most important for societies that
    A) had a highly developed system of writing
    B) were non-literate or had limited written records
    C) were under direct colonial rule
    D) engaged in the Transatlantic slave trade

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 1 explains that oral traditions are "Particularly important for understanding the history of societies that were non-literate or had limited written records."

206. The reliance of the ancient Ghana, Mali, and Songhai empires on the Trans-Saharan trade is economically analogous to modern Nigeria's reliance on
    A) agriculture
    B) the textile industry
    C) the export of crude oil
    D) foreign aid

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The Sudanic empires' wealth was heavily dependent on controlling the trade of a few key commodities (gold, salt). This creates an economy vulnerable to external shocks, similar to modern Nigeria's heavy dependence on the fluctuating world price of crude oil.

207. Both the Maji Maji Rebellion and the War of the Golden Stool were examples of
    A) successful expulsions of colonial powers
    B) primary armed resistance against European colonial rule
    C) nationalist movements using non-violent protest
    D) conflicts between two African empires

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 presents both as examples of early armed revolts by African peoples trying to resist or overthrow the imposition of colonial rule (Germans in East Africa, British in Asante).

208. The creation of the African Union (AU) to replace the Organization of African Unity (OAU) signified a shift towards
    A) greater willingness to intervene in the internal affairs of member states
    B) a stricter policy of non-interference under all circumstances
    C) a focus solely on economic matters
    D) dissolving all forms of continental cooperation

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Chapter 6 explains that a key difference was the AU's "Policy of Non-Indifference," a move away from the OAU's strict non-interference principle, allowing for intervention in grave circumstances.

209. A study of the impact of missionary schools on the rise of nationalism is a study of
    A) a planned outcome of colonial policy
    B) an unintended consequence of colonial-era activities
    C) the failure of African resistance
    D) the success of traditional education

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Colonial and missionary education (Chapter 8) was intended to produce clerks and catechists, not anti-colonial leaders. The fact that the educated elite used their knowledge to lead independence movements (Chapter 5) was an ironic and unintended outcome.

210. Which of the following lists events in the correct chronological order?
    A) Berlin Conference -> Independence of Nigeria -> Nigerian Civil War -> #EndSARS movement
    B) Independence of Nigeria -> Berlin Conference -> Nigerian Civil War -> #EndSARS movement
    C) Berlin Conference -> Nigerian Civil War -> Independence of Nigeria -> #EndSARS movement
    D) #EndSARS movement -> Nigerian Civil War -> Independence of Nigeria -> Berlin Conference

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Berlin Conference (1884-85), Independence of Nigeria (1960), Nigerian Civil War (1967-70), #EndSARS movement (2020).

211. Nationalist historiography would most likely focus on
    A) the achievements and heroic struggles of a particular nation for independence
    B) the economic exploitation of the working class during colonialism
    C) the historical experiences of women during the slave trade
    D) the impossibility of finding objective truth in historical narratives

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** Chapter 1 defines Nationalist Historiography as emphasizing "the history, identity, achievements, and struggles of a particular nation or national group," often to "promote national pride."

212. The need for "corroboration" in historical research is most evident when a historian is using
    A) a modern, peer-reviewed textbook
    B) a single, biased primary source document
    C) an official government census report
    D) an archaeological dating report

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** When a source is known to be biased (as discussed in Chapter 13), it is essential to corroborate its claims by comparing it with other sources to get a more balanced picture.

213. The role of the Oba in the Benin Kingdom was both
    A) economic and social
    B) religious and artistic
    C) political and spiritual
    D) colonial and traditional

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 2 describes the Oba as a "divine king with both political and spiritual authority," a common model for powerful monarchs in the region.

214. The development of Creole languages in the Americas is a direct cultural legacy of the
    A) Scramble for Africa
    B) Transatlantic Slave Trade
    C) Trans-Saharan Trade
    D) spread of Islam

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 lists the development of Creole languages as a key example of "Cultural Exchange and Syncretism" resulting from the African diaspora created by the slave trade.

215. The British policy of Indirect Rule most directly impacted which traditional institution?
    A) Age-grade systems
    B) Kinship and lineage
    C) Chieftaincy institutions
    D) Religious rituals

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Indirect Rule (Chapter 4) was a system of governance that worked directly through traditional rulers (chiefs, emirs), thus fundamentally altering the nature and power of chieftaincy institutions.

216. The annulment of the 1993 Nigerian election and the 1966 coup against Nkrumah are both examples of
    A) successful democratic transitions
    B) foreign military interventions
    C) the subversion of political processes by the military or ruling power
    D) popular grassroots revolutions

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** In both cases (Chapter 6), an expected or existing political order was abruptly overturned by a powerful internal group (the military), subverting the democratic or established process.

217. A historian studying the history of West African agriculture would be most interested in the
    A) shift from food crops to cash crops during colonialism
    B) development of bronze casting in Ife
    C) political structure of the Oyo Mesi
    D) use of social media in the #EndSARS protest

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** The shift from subsistence farming to the cultivation of cash crops like cocoa and palm oil (Chapter 4) was a fundamental change in the agricultural history of the region.

218. The argument that colonialism brought some benefits like infrastructure and Western medicine is often countered by the fact that
    A) these benefits were distributed equally among the population
    B) these benefits were primarily intended to serve colonial interests
    C) Africa had superior infrastructure and medicine before colonialism
    D) these benefits were rejected by all Africans

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 makes this point clearly, stating that infrastructure was built to extract resources and healthcare often prioritized European needs or the labor force, rather than for the general welfare of Africans.

219. The Fulani Jihad led by Usman dan Fodio had a similar effect to the Mfecane in Southern Africa in that both
    A) were peaceful religious movements
    B) led to large-scale political disruption and the formation of new, larger states
    C) were direct resistance movements against European colonialism
    D) resulted in the decline of trade and urbanization

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** The Fulani Jihad (Chapter 2) overthrew the Hausa city-states and created the large Sokoto Caliphate. The Mfecane (not in this text, but a common comparison) was a period of widespread warfare and political consolidation in Southern Africa. Both were transformative, state-building conflicts.

220. The work of a diviner in traditional Yoruba society and the work of an archivist in a modern state both involve
    A) interpreting evidence to provide information and insight
    B) communicating directly with supernatural beings
    C) preserving only written documents
    D) overthrowing the government

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** A diviner (Chapter 7) interprets signs to gain insight into problems. An archivist (Chapter 13) organizes and helps others interpret historical documents to gain insight into the past. Both are roles centered on interpretation of evidence within their respective systems.

221. Which product's trade was central to the economy of the Old Kingdom of Egypt?
    A) Gold
    B) Salt
    C) Grain (wheat, barley)
    D) Bronze

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text (Chapter 2) emphasizes that the Egyptian economy was based on agriculture, made possible by the Nile's fertile silt, with key crops being wheat and barley. This grain surplus supported the entire state structure.

222. The military strength of the Oyo Empire and the Kanem-Bornu Empire was significantly based on their
    A) powerful navies
    B) use of gunpowder firearms from the start
    C) effective use of cavalry
    D) large numbers of infantry archers

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** The text (Chapter 2) specifically notes that Oyo had a "strong military, especially renowned for its cavalry," and that for Kanem-Bornu, "cavalry was key."

223. The 13th Amendment in the USA and the Slavery Abolition Act of 1833 in Britain were both
    A) laws that started the slave trade
    B) legislative acts that abolished the institution of slavery
    C) economic policies to make slavery more profitable
    D) declarations of war against slave-trading nations

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 identifies the 1833 Act as abolishing slavery in the British Empire and the 13th Amendment (1865) as doing the same in the United States.

224. A key difference in the path to independence for Ghana and Nigeria was that
    A) Ghana's was violent while Nigeria's was peaceful
    B) Nigeria's involved negotiating a complex federal structure for its diverse regions
    C) Ghana was led by a council of elders while Nigeria was led by one charismatic leader
    D) Nigeria gained independence much earlier than Ghana

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 highlights the role of regional parties in Nigeria and the need for "negotiating the terms of federalism and regional autonomy," a more complex process than in the smaller, more centralized Gold Coast (Ghana).

225. The establishment of ECOWAS and the African Union are modern manifestations of the ideology of
    A) Neo-colonialism
    B) Pan-Africanism
    C) Social Darwinism
    D) The White Man's Burden

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Pan-Africanism (Chapter 5) is the ideology promoting African unity and solidarity. Regional and continental organizations like ECOWAS and the AU (Chapter 6) are practical attempts to implement that ideology.

226. What is a major limitation of using oral traditions as a historical source?
    A) They are always completely false
    B) They can be distorted during transmission over time
    C) They only exist in written form
    D) They reflect the views of European colonists

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 1 lists the limitations of oral traditions, including the "Potential for distortion, embellishment, or memory lapses during transmission over time."

227. The term "Middle Passage" refers specifically to the journey of
    A) goods from Europe to Africa
    B) enslaved Africans from Africa to the Americas
    C) raw materials from the Americas to Europe
    D) traders across the Sahara desert

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 explicitly defines the Middle Passage as the "brutal sea voyage from Africa to the Americas" for enslaved Africans.

228. Which of the following was a primary political motive for the European Scramble for Africa?
    A) The desire to learn from African systems of government
    B) The need to find soldiers for European wars
    C) The pressure to abolish the internal slave trade
    D) Competition and imperial rivalry among European nations for prestige

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 lists "Nationalism and Imperial Rivalry" as a key political motive, stating that "Colonies were seen as status symbols" in the competition for global power.

229. The first political party in Nigeria to have a broad, pan-Nigerian outlook was the
    A) Action Group (AG)
    B) Northern People's Congress (NPC)
    C) Nigerian National Democratic Party (NNDP)
    D) National Council of Nigeria and the Cameroons (NCNC)

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 describes the NCNC as "Initially a pan-Nigerian nationalist movement," in contrast to the more Lagos-based NNDP and the later, more regionally focused AG and NPC.

230. The military government of Gen. Yakubu Gowon is most associated with
    A) the First Republic and its collapse
    B) the prosecution of the Nigerian Civil War and post-war reconciliation
    C) the annulment of the June 12, 1993 election
    D) the transition to the Second Republic in 1979

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 6 clearly places Gowon as the Head of State during the Civil War (1967-70) and credits him with the "No Victor, No Vanquished" reconciliation policy.

231. Matrilineal descent was a feature of the kinship system among the
    A) Hausa
    B) Yoruba
    C) Akan
    D) Fulani

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 7 gives the "Akan in Ghana" as a specific example of a society with matrilineal descent.

232. In the context of post-colonial education, "Africanizing curricula" means
    A) teaching only in African languages
    B) making the content more relevant to African history, culture, and needs
    C) hiring only African teachers
    D) removing science and mathematics from schools

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 8 explains the goal was to reform the Eurocentric colonial curriculum to make it "more relevant to local culture, history, and needs."

233. The COVAX facility was created to address
    A) the digital divide in Africa
    B) the issue of climate change finance
    C) youth unemployment
    D) inequitable global access to COVID-19 vaccines

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Chapter 12 mentions the "COVAX facility aimed to provide equitable access" to vaccines, to counter the effects of vaccine nationalism.

234. In historical writing, providing a full citation for a source is essential to
    A) prove the historian is intelligent
    B) make the essay longer
    C) avoid plagiarism and allow for verification
    D) show respect for the publisher

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 13 stresses that citations are "Crucial for academic integrity and allowing readers to verify your sources," and for avoiding plagiarism.

235. The Bronze heads of Ife are artistically distinct from the Terracotta heads of Nok because the Ife heads are generally more
    A) abstract and stylized
    B) naturalistic and lifelike
    C) crudely made
    D) focused on animal figures

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 2 contrasts the "distinctive" and "stylized" art of Nok with the "highly naturalistic and sophisticated sculptures" of Ife, which were "idealized but lifelike."

236. The decline of the Oyo Empire in the 19th century was caused by a combination of internal power struggles and
    A) the Trans-Saharan trade
    B) the rise of the Benin Kingdom
    C) incursions from the Fulani jihad
    D) the discovery of gold

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 2 lists "Fulani incursions from the north" as one of the key factors contributing to Oyo's decline.

237. The economic theory that the profitability of Caribbean sugar plantations was declining, thus making the abolition of the slave trade easier for Britain, is known as the
    A) Adam Smith thesis
    B) Lord Lugard thesis
    C) Eric Williams thesis
    D) Amartya Sen thesis

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 3 directly attributes this argument to the "Eric Williams thesis."

238. Both Samori Ture and Menelik II are remembered as figures who
    A) collaborated with European colonialists
    B) led significant armed resistance against European invasion
    C) were key leaders in post-independence governments
    D) were famous traders on the Trans-Saharan route

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 presents both as key examples of "Primary Resistance," with Ture fighting the French and Menelik II successfully defeating the Italians at Adwa.

239. The All People's Congress (APC) and the Sierra Leone People's Party (SLPP) are the major historical political parties in
    A) Ghana
    B) Nigeria
    C) Sierra Leone
    D) The Gambia

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 lists both the SLPP and APC as the key political parties in the nationalist and post-colonial history of Sierra Leone.

240. The transition from the OAU to the AU reflected a change in principle from strict non-interference to
    A) a responsibility to protect and intervene in certain crises
    B) a focus on cultural matters only
    C) alignment with the Soviet Union in the Cold War
    D) the promotion of colonialism

    **Answer:** A
    **Explanation:** This shift is captured in the "Policy of Non-Indifference" (Chapter 6), which gives the AU a mandate to intervene in cases of war crimes or genocide, reflecting a "responsibility to protect."

241. A traditional ruler who holds land "in trust" for the community is acting as a
    A) private owner
    B) colonial agent
    C) custodian
    D) commercial seller

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 7 describes one of the roles of a traditional ruler as "Custodianship of land and resources on behalf of the community."

242. The "brain drain" is a significant challenge to which post-colonial development goal?
    A) Forging national unity
    B) Creating a skilled workforce and retaining it for national development
    C) Holding regular elections
    D) Maintaining traditional family structures

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 8 lists "brain drain" as a major challenge for post-colonial education, as it involves the loss of the very skilled professionals the new nations invested in creating.

243. The use of mobile money systems like M-Pesa is a prime example of
    A) the digital divide
    B) Fintech (Financial Technology) innovation in Africa
    C) a negative effect of globalization
    D) a government surveillance program

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 12 lists "Fintech (Financial Technology): Mobile money, digital payments..." as a key area in Africa's digital revolution.

244. When a historian checks a document for anachronisms (things out of place for the period), they are performing
    A) internal criticism
    B) synthesis
    C) external criticism
    D) historiography

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 1 defines external criticism (authenticity) as involving "checking for anachronisms."

245. The Bayajidda legend is the foundational myth for the
    A) Yoruba people
    B) Benin Kingdom
    C) Hausa states
    D) Asante Empire

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 2 mentions that the origins of the Hausa states are explained by "tradition (Bayajidda legend)."

246. The large-scale humanitarian crisis and starvation caused by a federal blockade during the Nigerian Civil War occurred in
    A) the Western Region
    B) the Northern Region
    C) Lagos
    D) Biafra

    **Answer:** D
    **Explanation:** Chapter 6 clearly states there was "Widespread suffering, starvation, and humanitarian crisis in Biafra due to blockade."

247. The introduction of cash crop economies during the colonial period often led to
    A) increased food security
    B) the decline of food crop production for local consumption
    C) the growth of local manufacturing
    D) a decrease in exports to Europe

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** Chapter 4 notes that cash crop production was "sometimes at the expense of food crop production for local consumption," which could lead to food insecurity.

248. The political party most associated with Nnamdi Azikiwe and the Igbo people of Eastern Nigeria was the
    A) AG
    B) NPC
    C) NCNC
    D) CPP

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 5 describes the NCNC as being "later led by Nnamdi Azikiwe" and becoming "more dominant in Eastern Nigeria."

249. The establishment of Islamic centers of learning with mosques and universities in cities like Timbuktu and Djenné was a direct result of the
    A) Transatlantic Slave Trade
    B) European colonial conquest
    C) Trans-Saharan Trade
    D) Nok Civilization

    **Answer:** C
    **Explanation:** Chapter 2 links the Trans-Saharan trade to the "Spread of Islam" and the "establishment of Islamic centers of learning (e.g., Timbuktu, Djenné)."

250. A historian writing a biography of Yaa Asantewaa is contributing to which two fields of history?
    A) Economic and Diplomatic History
    B) Women's History and Political History
    C) Intellectual History and Cultural History
    D) Social History and Environmental History

    **Answer:** B
    **Explanation:** As a woman who led a major military and political resistance movement, a biography of Yaa Asantewaa would be a key text in both Women's History (recovering female experiences) and Political History (the study of resistance, power, and conflict).


Based on the comprehensive textbook content you provided, here are 250 WAEC standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations.

### Chapter 1: Introduction to History and Historiography (15 Questions)

**1. According to the text, the Greek word 'historia', from which 'history' is derived, means**
A. a collection of dates
B. the study of great men
C. inquiry, knowledge acquired by investigation
D. a record of what happened
E. narration of past events
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text explicitly states, "History (from Greek historia, meaning 'inquiry, knowledge acquired by investigation') is the study of the past..."

**2. Which branch of history focuses on the everyday lives, social structures, and experiences of ordinary people?**
A. Political History
B. Economic History
C. Cultural History
D. Social History
E. Diplomatic History
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text defines Social History as focusing on "the everyday lives, experiences, social structures, customs, and relationships of ordinary people in the past."

**3. A history textbook written by a modern historian is an example of a**
A. primary source
B. secondary source
C. oral tradition
D. archaeological artifact
E. visual source
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text defines secondary sources as "accounts or interpretations of past events created by someone who was not a direct witness or participant" and gives history textbooks as a specific example.

**4. The process of checking a historical source for anachronisms and determining if it is genuine is known as**
A. internal criticism
B. contextualization
C. external criticism
D. interpretation
E. historiography
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** External Criticism is described as asking "Is the source what it claims to be? Is it genuine or a forgery/fabrication?" and involves checking for anachronisms.

**5. Which school of historiography emphasizes economic factors and class struggle as the primary drivers of historical change?**
A. Nationalist Historiography
B. Feminist Historiography
C. Revisionist Historiography
D. Afrocentric Historiography
E. Marxist Historiography
**Answer: E**
**Explanation:** The text states that Marxist Historiography "Emphasizes economic factors, class struggle, and modes of production as the primary drivers of historical change."

**6. Judging past events by present-day standards is a historical error known as**
A. chronology
B. contextualization
C. corroboration
D. anachronism
E. synthesis
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text, under Contextualization, warns to "Avoid anachronism (judging past events or people by present-day standards, values, or knowledge that would not have been available to them)."

**7. A key advantage of written records over oral traditions as a historical source is their**
A. ability to capture non-elite perspectives
B. dynamism and adaptability
C. relative stability and precision over time
D. freedom from bias and propaganda
E. performance-based nature
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text lists an advantage of written records as their ability to "provide detailed, precise, and relatively stable information over long periods (if preserved)."

**8. Griots in West Africa are primarily known as**
A. custodians of oral traditions
B. military commanders
C. merchant traders
D. political rulers
E. religious priests
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The text gives "griots in West Africa" as an example of specific individuals responsible for preserving and transmitting oral traditions.

**9. Afrocentric historiography primarily aims to**
A. prove the superiority of African civilizations
B. challenge Eurocentric biases and re-center history on African perspectives
C. focus exclusively on the history of the African diaspora
D. revise traditional narratives using postmodern theories
E. analyze history through the lens of national pride
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text explains that Afrocentric Historiography aims to "re-center historical narratives from the perspective of African peoples... challenging Eurocentric biases."

**10. A letter written by a soldier during a war is a classic example of a**
A. secondary source
B. tertiary source
C. primary source
D. revisionist history
E. historiographical analysis
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text lists "Letters, diaries, journals" as prime examples of written primary sources because they are original accounts from the period being studied.

**11. The study of the history of historical writing and interpretation is called**
A. archaeology
B. anthropology
C. chronology
D. historiography
E. diplomacy
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text explicitly defines Historiography as "The study of the writing of history; the history of historical thought and interpretation."

**12. All of the following are listed as reasons to study history EXCEPT**
A. developing critical thinking skills
B. learning from the past
C. guaranteeing future success
D. understanding the present
E. cultivating empathy and perspective
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text lists many benefits of studying history, but it does not claim that it can guarantee future success; rather, it can "inform current decision-making."

**13. The Annales School of historiography is known for its focus on**
A. political events and great leaders
B. inevitable progress towards liberty
C. long-term social and economic structures and everyday life
D. the history of ideas and intellectuals
E. class struggle and modes of production
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text mentions the Annales School as focusing on "long-term social and economic structures, mentalities, and everyday life, rather than just political events."

**14. When a historian compares information from multiple sources to check for consistency, they are engaging in**
A. internal criticism
B. anachronism
C. revisionism
D. corroboration
E. synthesis
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text defines corroboration under Internal Criticism as "Comparing information from multiple sources to check for consistency or discrepancies."

**15. Feminist historiography is primarily concerned with**
A. recovering the historical experiences of women and analyzing gender roles
B. celebrating the achievements of a specific nation
C. proving that women are superior to men
D. studying the history of warfare from a female perspective
E. challenging Marxist interpretations of history
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The text states that Feminist Historiography "aims to recover and analyze the historical experiences, roles, and contributions of women" and "Examines how gender... has shaped historical processes."

### Chapter 2: Early Civilizations in Africa (25 Questions)

**16. The Nok civilization, one of the earliest complex societies in West Africa, is renowned for its**
A. bronze head sculptures
B. massive stone pyramids
C. terracotta sculptures
D. hieroglyphic writing
E. walled cities
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text clearly states the Nok civilization is "Best known for its distinctive terracotta sculptures."

**17. The highly naturalistic and idealized bronze heads of royalty are a famous artistic achievement of the**
A. Nok Civilization
B. Ife Civilization
C. Kanem-Bornu Empire
D. Benin Kingdom
E. Oyo Empire
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Ife Civilization is described as "Renowned for its highly naturalistic and sophisticated sculptures in terracotta, bronze... representing royalty (Onis or kings)."

**18. What technological innovation was central to the Nok civilization's development?**
A. Lost-wax casting
B. Iron smelting
C. Chariot construction
D. Papyrus making
E. Irrigation systems
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text highlights that the Nok had "Evidence of early iron smelting technology... placing them among the earliest iron-using cultures in Africa."

**19. The Benin Expedition of 1897 led to the**
A. establishment of the Benin Empire
B. introduction of Christianity to Benin
C. looting of many Benin bronzes by the British
D. signing of a major trade treaty with Portugal
E. decline of the Trans-Saharan trade
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text mentions that "Many Benin bronzes were looted by the British during the Benin Expedition of 1897 and are now in museums worldwide."

**20. The ancient Egyptian civilization was heavily dependent on which river?**
A. Niger
B. Congo
C. Zambezi
D. Nile
E. Senegal
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text quotes Herodotus, stating, "Egypt is the gift of the Nile," and explains the civilization's dependency on it for agriculture and transport.

**21. The ruler of ancient Egypt, considered a divine king, was known as the**
A. Oba
B. Sarki
C. Oni
D. Alaafin
E. Pharaoh
**Answer: E**
**Explanation:** The text defines Pharaohs as "The rulers of ancient Egypt. Considered divine or semi-divine kings."

**22. The Great Pyramids of Giza were primarily built during the**
A. New Kingdom
B. Old Kingdom
C. Middle Kingdom
D. Late Period
E. Predynastic Period
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The Old Kingdom is explicitly referred to as the "'Age of Pyramids,'" and the text states pyramids were primarily built as tombs for pharaohs during this period.

**23. In ancient Egyptian religion, the concept of cosmic order, justice, and harmony was known as**
A. Ma'at
B. Osiris
C. Ra
D. Anubis
E. Horus
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The text states the pharaoh was responsible for "maintaining Ma'at (cosmic order, justice, harmony)."

**24. What were the two most important commodities exchanged along the Trans-Saharan trade routes?**
A. Gold and ivory
B. Salt and textiles
C. Gold and salt
D. Slaves and horses
E. Kola nuts and dates
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text emphasizes the trade of gold from West Africa and salt from the Sahara, noting they were "Often traded ounce for ounce."

**25. The introduction of which animal was crucial for the expansion of the Trans-Saharan trade?**
A. Horse
B. Donkey
C. Ox
D. Camel
E. Elephant
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text identifies camels as the primary means of transport, calling them "'ships of the desert'" due to their adaptation to desert conditions.

**26. The spread of which religion was a major consequence of the Trans-Saharan trade?**
A. Christianity
B. Judaism
C. Buddhism
D. Hinduism
E. Islam
**Answer: E**
**Explanation:** A key impact of the trade was the "Spread of Islam," as Arab and Berber traders brought the religion to West Africa.

**27. Kano and Katsina were major commercial city-states belonging to which group?**
A. Yoruba
B. Fulani
C. Asante
D. Hausa
E. Edo
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text identifies Kano and Katsina as original Hausa states (Hausa Bakwai) and major commercial centers in the Trans-Saharan trade.

**28. The long-lasting Kanem-Bornu Empire was centered around**
A. the Jos Plateau
B. the Futa Jallon highlands
C. Lake Chad
D. the Niger Delta
E. the Volta River basin
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text locates the Kanem-Bornu Empire as being "Centered around Lake Chad."

**29. The political system of the Oyo Empire was characterized by**
A. absolute power of the Alaafin
B. a council of chiefs called the Oyo Mesi that could check the king's power
C. rule by a succession of military generals
D. a democratic system with universal suffrage
E. a diarchy of a political and a religious leader
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text describes the Oyo Mesi as a "Council of seven principal non-royal chiefs, responsible for selecting/installing the Alaafin and could also depose him," serving as a check on his power.

**30. The early 19th-century jihad led by Usman dan Fodio resulted in the establishment of the**
A. Oyo Empire
B. Asante Kingdom
C. Sokoto Caliphate
D. Mali Empire
E. Kanem-Bornu Empire
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text states that Usman dan Fodio's jihad "established the Sokoto Caliphate, a large Islamic empire encompassing Hausaland and beyond."

**31. The lost-wax casting method (cire perdue) was a sophisticated technique used in the art of**
A. Nok only
B. Egypt and Nok
C. Ife and Benin
D. Oyo and Hausaland
E. Kanem-Bornu only
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text explicitly mentions that both Ife and Benin civilizations were renowned for their use of the lost-wax casting method for their bronze sculptures.

**32. The vizier in ancient Egypt served as the**
A. chief priest of the temple of Ra
B. commander of the army
C. governor of a province
D. chief minister and second in command to the pharaoh
E. head of the royal treasury
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text defines the Vizier as the "Chief minister, second in command to pharaoh, who oversaw administration."

**33. The main purpose of mummification in ancient Egypt was to**
A. display the pharaoh's wealth
B. preserve the body for the afterlife
C. prevent the spread of disease
D. study human anatomy
E. create religious icons for worship
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text explains mummification was driven by the "Belief in afterlife and need to preserve the pharaoh's body... for his journey to the next world."

**34. Timbuktu, Gao, and Djenné were major southern terminus cities for the**
A. Atlantic slave trade
B. Indian Ocean trade
C. Trans-Saharan trade
D. Mediterranean sea trade
E. Red Sea trade
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text lists Timbuktu, Gao, and Djenné as key "Southern Termini (Sahelian cities)" of the Trans-Saharan trade routes.

**35. The military strength of the Oyo Empire was particularly renowned for its**
A. navy
B. archers
C. long-spearmen
D. cavalry
E. siege engines
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text notes the Oyo Empire had a "Strong military, especially renowned for its cavalry, which allowed for expansion."

**36. The Bayajidda legend is the traditional origin story for the**
A. Oyo Empire
B. Yoruba people
C. Hausa states
D. Kingdom of Benin
E. Nok civilization
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The text mentions that according to the "Bayajidda legend," the seven original Hausa states were founded.

**37. The Sefuwa dynasty was one of Africa's longest-ruling dynasties, associated with the**
A. Hausa states
B. Oyo Empire
C. Benin Kingdom
D. Kanem-Bornu Empire
E. Mali Empire
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text states that the Kanem-Bornu Empire was "Ruled by the Mai (king) of the Sefuwa dynasty (one of Africa's longest ruling dynasties)."

**38. The spiritual and ancestral homeland of the Yoruba people is considered to be**
A. Benin City
B. Kano
C. Oyo-Ile
D. Ife
E. Nok
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The text identifies Ife as the "ancestral and spiritual homeland of the Yoruba people."

**39. In the Oyo Empire, the Ogboni Society acted as a further check on the power of the**
A. common people
B. army commander
C. neighboring states
D. merchant class
E. Alaafin and the Oyo Mesi
**Answer: E**
**Explanation:** The text describes the Ogboni Society as a powerful group that "acted as a further check on Alaafin and Oyo Mesi."

**40. A major reason for the decline of the Trans-Saharan trade from the late 16th century was the**
A. discovery of new gold mines in the Sahara
B. rise of European maritime trade along the West African coast
C. conversion of West African rulers to Christianity
D. exhaustion of the Saharan salt mines
E. collapse of the Ottoman Empire
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The text lists the "rise of European maritime trade along the West African coast (shifting trade focus to Atlantic)" as a key factor in the decline of the Trans-Saharan trade.
