Explanation: Globalization is defined in the text as this process of increasing interconnectedness.

132. The rapid and widespread adoption of which technology has been particularly transformative across Africa in the 21st century?
A. Landline telephones
B. Personal computers
C. Mobile phones
D. Fax machines
Answer: C
Explanation: The text highlights the "transformative" impact of mobile phone penetration for communication, mobile banking, and access to information.

133. The disparity in access to the internet and digital technologies within and between countries is referred to as the
A. Digital revolution.
B. Digital divide.
C. Digital economy.
D. Digital footprint.
Answer: B
Explanation: The "digital divide" is the term used to describe the significant disparities in internet access, often between urban and rural areas or different income levels.

1<strong>34. M-Pesa in East Africa is a pioneering example of successful African innovation in the field of
A. Agricultural Technology (AgriTech).
B. Education Technology (EdTech).
C. Financial Technology (Fintech).
D. E-commerce.
Answer: C
Explanation: M-Pesa, a mobile money service, is a prime example of Fintech, which the text mentions as a key part of Africa's digital revolution.

135. During the COVID-19 pandemic, a major challenge faced by African nations in obtaining vaccines was
A. a lack of demand for vaccines.
B. global disparities in access, often called "vaccine nationalism."
C. the refusal of the Africa CDC to participate.
D. the high effectiveness of traditional cures.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text points to "vaccine nationalism" by wealthier nations and global competition as a significant hurdle for African countries in accessing vaccines equitably.

136. The coordinating body for the continent's pandemic response, which played a key role during the COVID-19 crisis, is the
A. World Health Organization (WHO).
B. Africa CDC (Centres for Disease Control and Prevention).
C. ECOWAS Health Organisation.
D. African Union Peace and Security Council.
Answer: B
Explanation: The Africa CDC is specifically mentioned as having played a crucial coordinating role in the continent's public health response to the pandemic.

137. The #EndSARS movement in Nigeria in 2020 was a youth-led protest primarily against
A. high unemployment rates.
B. police brutality.
C. the annulment of an election.
D. climate change.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text clearly states that the #EndSARS movement was initially a protest against police brutality by the Special Anti-Robbery Squad (SARS).

138. The #BringBackOurGirls movement, which gained global attention, was an advocacy campaign demanding
A. an end to gender inequality in politics.
B. the rescue of schoolgirls abducted by Boko Haram.
C. free primary education for all girls.
D. reforms in the Nigerian military.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text identifies #BringBackOurGirls as a campaign demanding the rescue of the schoolgirls who were abducted from Chibok by Boko Haram.

139. A key characteristic of 21st-century youth movements like #EndSARS is their extensive use of
A. traditional media like radio.
B. door-to-door campaigning.
C. social media for mobilization.
D. international court systems.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text emphasizes that these movements leveraged technology, mobilizing extensively through social media platforms like Twitter and Instagram.

140. The set of 17 global goals adopted by the UN in 2015 to end poverty, protect the planet, and ensure prosperity for all is known as the
A. Millennium Development Goals (MDGs).
B. Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).
C. Paris Agreement.
D. Agenda 2063.
Answer: B
Explanation: The Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) are defined as the 17 goals adopted in 2015 as part of the 2030 Agenda.

141. A major reason Africa is considered highly vulnerable to climate change is that
A. it is the world's largest historical emitter of greenhouse gases.
B. many of its economies are heavily reliant on climate-sensitive sectors like agriculture.
C. it lacks any renewable energy potential.
D. its population is the oldest in the world.
Answer: B
Explanation: Africa's vulnerability stems from factors like its reliance on agriculture, which is highly sensitive to changes in temperature and rainfall, as mentioned in the text's description of climate impacts.

142. Efforts by African countries to build resilience against the impacts of climate change, such as using drought-resistant crops, are known as
A. Mitigation measures.
B. Adaptation measures.
C. Geo-engineering.
D. Carbon offsetting.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text defines adaptation as the focus on building resilience to climate impacts, providing examples like using drought-resistant crops and water harvesting.

143. Investing in solar, wind, and geothermal power are examples of which type of climate action?
A. Adaptation
B. Mitigation
C. Sequestration
D. Resilience
Answer: B
Explanation: Mitigation measures are efforts to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, and the text lists investing in renewable energy like solar and wind as a key example.

144. In international climate negotiations, African countries often advocate for increased financial support from
A. other developing nations.
B. the World Bank exclusively.
C. developed countries who are historically larger emitters.
D. private corporations only.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text states that African nations advocate for increased climate finance from developed countries, which have a greater historical responsibility for emissions.

15. The "Y'en a Marre" ("Fed Up") movement in Senegal is an example of a
A. religious revivalist group.
B. youth-led civil society movement promoting democratic transition.
C. secessionist movement.
D. pro-government rally.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text cites "Y'en a Marre" as an example of a youth-led movement that protested against a president's attempt to extend his rule, contributing to a democratic transition.

Chapter 13: Approaches to Historical Research

146. In historical research, a university professor's book about the Asante Empire is considered a
A. Primary source.
B. Secondary source.
C. Tertiary source.
D. Raw data.
Answer: B
Explanation: As per the definitions in Chapter 1 and reviewed in Chapter 13, a scholarly book written by a historian analyzing past events is a quintessential secondary source.

147. When a researcher checks if a historical document is genuine or a forgery, they are conducting
A. Internal criticism.
B. Historiographical analysis.
C. External criticism.
D. Contextual analysis.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 13 reviews the concept from Chapter 1, where External Criticism is the process of establishing the authenticity of a source.

148. The primary purpose of an oral history interview is to
A. record ancient myths and legends.
B. collect personal experiences and memories from an individual's lifetime.
C. analyze the literary quality of a person's speech.
D. create a legally binding testimony.
Answer: B
Explanation: The text distinguishes oral history from oral tradition, defining it as a method for collecting information about an individual's own personal experiences and recollections.

149. The process of converting a recorded interview into a written text is called
A. Transcription.
B. Translation.
C. Analysis.
D. Citation.
Answer: A
Explanation: Transcription is defined as the process of creating a written text from an audio or video recording of an interview.

150. A crucial ethical consideration in oral history is obtaining an interviewee's permission after fully explaining the project's purpose. This is known as
A. Informed consent.
B. Peer review.
C. Scholarly review.
D. Copyright.
Answer: A
Explanation: The text emphasizes that informed consent, where the interviewee fully understands the project and their rights, is a key ethical principle.

151. Official government records, such as legislative documents and census data, are typically held in a
A. Public library.
B. University archive.
C. National archive.
D. Private collection.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text specifies that National Archives are the repositories for the official records of a country's government.

152. Finding aids in an archive serve the purpose of
A. helping researchers handle documents carefully.
B. providing a quiet place for research.
C. describing the archive's collections to help locate records.
D. translating documents into different languages.
Answer: C
Explanation: Finding aids are described as catalogs, indexes, and inventories that help researchers navigate the collections and find relevant materials.

153. A clear, specific, and arguable statement that presents a writer's main interpretation in a historical essay is the
A. Introduction.
B. Conclusion.
C. Thesis statement.
D. Topic sentence.
Answer: C
Explanation: The thesis statement is defined as the central, arguable claim or interpretation that the entire essay aims to support with evidence.

154. Using someone else's words or ideas without proper attribution in an academic essay is
A. called citation.
B. a good research practice.
C. called plagiarism.
D. a form of internal criticism.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text explicitly defines plagiarism as using another's work without proper attribution and calls it a serious academic offense.

155. The Chicago Manual of Style is mentioned as a common
A. historical theory.
B. type of archive.
C. source of historical data.
D. citation style used in history writing.
Answer: D
Explanation: The text lists the Chicago Manual of Style as a common citation style used by historians for footnotes, endnotes, and bibliographies.

156. The first step in conducting archival research is to
A. request materials immediately.
B. take detailed notes.
C. identify which archives are likely to hold relevant records.
D. handle documents with care.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text outlines the first step as identifying the relevant archives for one's specific research topic before planning a visit or accessing materials.

157. An "open-ended question" is a recommended technique in oral history because it
A. can be answered with a simple "yes" or "no".
B. encourages the interviewee to provide detailed, narrative answers.
C. leads the interviewee to a specific, desired answer.
D. tests the interviewee's memory of specific dates.
Answer: B
Explanation: Open-ended questions (e.g., "Tell me about...") are recommended to encourage the narrator to speak at length and share their story in their own words.

158. The process of placing a historical source within its broader social, cultural, and political circumstances is known as
A. Corroboration.
B. Contextualization.
C. Transcription.
D. Authentication.
Answer: B
Explanation: Contextualization, a key historical skill reviewed in Chapter 13, involves understanding a source by placing it in its proper historical setting.

159. When writing a historical essay, a body paragraph should ideally begin with a
A. thesis statement.
B. direct quotation.
C. topic sentence.
D. concluding thought.
Answer: C
Explanation: The text advises that each body paragraph should start with a clear topic sentence that introduces the specific point that paragraph will develop in support of the main thesis.

160. Which of the following is an example of a primary source for a historian studying the Nigerian Civil War?
A. A 2010 book analyzing the causes of the war.
B. A diary kept by a civilian in Biafra during the war.
C. A university lecture on General Gowon's policies.
D. An encyclopedia entry on the war.
Answer: B
Explanation: A diary kept by a participant or witness is a direct, firsthand account and therefore a primary source, as per the definition. The other options are secondary sources.

Continuing to generate questions to reach 250...

Additional Questions (Chapters 1-8, 12-13)

161. A historian who focuses on the history of ideas and influential thinkers is specializing in
A. Military History.
B. Diplomatic History.
C. Intellectual History.
D. Oral History.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 1 defines Intellectual History as the branch focusing on the history of ideas, thinkers, and intellectual movements.

162. Judging past events by present-day standards and values is a historical fallacy known as
A. Anachronism.
B. Revisionism.
C. Corroboration.
D. Synthesis.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 1 warns against anachronism, which is judging the past by standards and knowledge that would not have been available to people at the time.

163. The Annales School of historiography is known for its focus on
A. the actions of great political leaders.
B. long-term social and economic structures and everyday life.
C. the inevitable march of progress towards liberty.
D. challenging the possibility of objective historical truth.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 1 briefly mentions the Annales School as focusing on long-term structures ("longue durée"), mentalities, and everyday life, rather than just political events.

164. The lost-wax casting method (cire perdue) was a sophisticated technique used in the art of
A. Nok and Ancient Egypt.
B. Ife and Benin.
C. Kanem-Bornu and Hausaland.
D. Oyo and Songhai.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 highlights the use of the lost-wax casting method for the famous bronze sculptures of both Ife and Benin.

165. The chief minister and second in command to the pharaoh in ancient Egypt was the
A. Nomarch.
B. Vizier.
C. Scribe.
D. Priest.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 identifies the Vizier as the chief minister who oversaw the complex bureaucracy of the ancient Egyptian state.

166. In the Trans-Saharan trade, the Berber and Tuareg peoples of the Sahara played a crucial role as
A. gold miners.
B. salt producers.
C. slaves.
D. traders, guides, and controllers of oases.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 2 states that Berber and Tuareg nomads were essential intermediaries in the trade, serving as guides and traders across the desert.

167. The decline of the Trans-Saharan trade from the late 16th century was partly caused by the
A. rise of European maritime trade along the West African coast.
B. depletion of all salt mines in the Sahara.
C. spread of Islam, which discouraged trade.
D. unification of all West African states.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 2 explains that the decline was linked to the shift in trade focus to the Atlantic coast due to the arrival of European maritime traders.

168. The Ogboni Society in the Oyo Empire was a
A. council of seven chiefs that selected the king.
B. powerful secret society of elders that acted as a check on power.
C. group of elite cavalry warriors.
D. guild of long-distance traders.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 describes the Ogboni Society as a powerful group involved in religious and political matters that served as a further check on the power of the Alaafin and the Oyo Mesi.

169. Before the large-scale external slave trades, forms of servitude in Africa were often caused by
A. racial ideology.
B. warfare, debt, or punishment for crimes.
C. the demand for plantation labor.
D. European influence.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 3 notes that pre-existing forms of slavery in some African societies were often the result of factors like warfare (prisoners), debt, or crime.

170. The "Eric Williams thesis" controversially argues that the abolition of the slave trade was partly driven by
A. purely moral and humanitarian concerns.
B. the declining profitability of slave-based economies.
C. the success of the Haitian Revolution alone.
D. a desire to punish the American colonies.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 3 mentions the debated Eric Williams thesis, which suggests that economic factors, such as the declining profitability of British Caribbean plantations, made abolition more palatable.

171. The last country in the Americas to abolish slavery, in 1888, was
A. The United States.
B. Cuba.
C. Great Britain.
D. Brazil.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 3 states that Brazil was the last country in the Americas to abolish the institution of slavery, in 1888.

172. The use of the Maxim gun was a key technological advantage that facilitated
A. the Trans-Saharan trade.
B. the European conquest and penetration of the African interior.
C. the expansion of the Oyo Empire.
D. the abolition of the slave trade.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 4 lists improved weaponry, specifically the Maxim gun, as a technological factor that gave Europeans a military advantage during the Scramble for Africa.

173. The colonial practice of seizing African land for European settlers was known as
A. Land alienation.
B. Land reform.
C. Land tenure.
D. Land development.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 4 defines "Land alienation" as the expropriation of land from Africans for use by European settlers or for large-scale plantations.

174. The Maji Maji Rebellion (1905-1907) was a major uprising against colonial rule in
A. German East Africa.
B. French West Africa.
C. British Nigeria.
D. Belgian Congo.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 4 cites the Maji Maji Rebellion as an example of an early revolt against colonial rule, specifically in German East Africa (modern-day Tanzania).

175. The West African Students' Union (WASU) and the National Congress of British West Africa (NCBWA) are examples of
A. mass-based political parties demanding immediate independence.
B. early nationalist activities focused on demanding reforms.
C. traditional religious movements.
D. European-led welfare organizations.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 5 mentions these groups as examples of early nationalist activities that focused on reforms and greater African participation in government rather than immediate independence.

176. At Nigeria's independence, the federal government was formed by a coalition of which two parties?
A. AG and NPC
B. NCNC and AG
C. NPC and NCNC
D. CPP and NPC
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 5 states that the first federal government was a coalition of the Northern People's Congress (NPC) and the National Council of Nigeria and the Cameroons (NCNC).

177. A major economic project undertaken by Nkrumah's government in Ghana was the
A. construction of a new capital city.
B. privatization of the cocoa industry.
C. Volta River Project and the Akosombo Dam.
D. development of the Trans-Saharan railway.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6 lists the Volta River Project, including the Akosombo Dam for hydroelectric power, as a major state-led development project under Nkrumah.

178. The unification decree that abolished Nigeria's federal structure in 1966 and contributed to the counter-coup was issued by
A. Tafawa Balewa.
B. Yakubu Gowon.
C. Johnson Aguiyi-Ironsi.
D. Nnamdi Azikiwe.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6 mentions that General Ironsi's unification decree, which abolished federalism, was seen as a threat by the North and was a factor leading to the July 1966 counter-coup.

179. The execution of Ken Saro-Wiwa and other Ogoni activists, which led to international isolation, occurred under the Nigerian military regime of
A. Ibrahim Babangida.
B. Sani Abacha.
C. Muhammadu Buhari.
D. Yakubu Gowon.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 6 identifies the highly authoritarian regime of Sani Abacha as being responsible for the execution of Ken Saro-Wiwa and the Ogoni Nine.

180. The practice of pouring drinks as an offering to ancestors or spirits is a ritual known as
A. Divination.
B. Libation.
C. Sacrifice.
D. Initiation.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 7 mentions libations (pouring drinks) as a common ritual used to honor ancestors in traditional African religions.

181. In traditional Yoruba religion, the god of thunder is
A. Ogun.
B. Sango.
C. Yemoja.
D. Olodumare.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 7 uses Sango as an example of an Orisha (a lesser divinity) associated with a natural force, in this case, thunder.

182. The Yoruba system of divination, which is complex and requires specialized training, is called
A. Voodoo.
B. Juju.
C. Ifa.
D. Orisha.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 7 gives Ifa divination as a specific example of a sophisticated divination practice among the Yoruba.

183. A key function of masquerades in many West African societies is to
A. provide simple entertainment for children.
B. represent spirits or ancestral figures in community rituals.
C. serve as a form of currency.
D. elect political leaders.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 7 explains that masks are part of a performance (masquerade) that often represents spirits or ancestors during important community rituals.

184. The Kora, Balafon, and Ngoni are all examples of West African
A. drums.
B. traditional textiles.
C. spoken art forms.
D. musical instruments.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 7 lists the Kora (harp-lute), Balafon (xylophone), and Ngoni (lute) as examples of traditional West African musical instruments.

185. A major challenge of post-colonial urbanization is the strain on
A. traditional kinship systems only.
B. rural farming.
C. national unity.
D. urban infrastructure and services.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 8 states that rapid urban growth puts a significant strain on infrastructure and services like water, sanitation, electricity, and transport.

186. The French colonial policy that came after "assimilation" and was supposedly more respectful of local cultures, though still asserting French superiority, was called
A. Indirect Rule.
B. Association.
C. Paternalism.
D. Direct Rule.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 4 mentions that the French later moved from a policy of "assimilation" to one of "association."

187. "Nollywood" is a term referring to the prolific film industry of
A. Ghana.
B. Senegal.
C. Nigeria.
D. South Africa.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 12 mentions Nollywood streaming as an example of Africa's creative industries thriving in the digital age. Nigeria's film industry is famously known as Nollywood.

188. A key challenge for youth movements like #EndSARS is
A. a lack of social media presence.
B. gaining international attention.
C. state repression and achieving sustainable, long-term reform.
D. finding a clear initial grievance to protest against.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 12 notes that while these movements are effective at mobilization, they often face challenges such as state repression and difficulties in ensuring their demands lead to lasting systemic change.

189. A primary source must be critically evaluated for its author's
A. writing style and grammar.
B. length and complexity.
C. bias, perspective, and purpose.
D. potential for digitization.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapters 1 and 13 both emphasize that a crucial part of source criticism is to evaluate the creator's potential biases, their perspective, and their reason for creating the source.

190. Which of the following best describes the historical process of synthesis?
A. Arranging events in chronological order
B. Identifying a single cause for a historical event
C. Combining information from various sources into a coherent historical account
D. Proving a historical source is authentic
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 1 describes synthesis as the final stage of historical inquiry where historians combine and synthesize information from multiple sources to construct a coherent narrative or explanation.

191. A key contribution of Feminist Historiography is
A. focusing exclusively on the history of male leaders.
B. recovering the historical experiences of women that were often overlooked.
C. arguing that economic factors are the only driver of history.
D. promoting a heroic version of a nation's past.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 1 defines Feminist Historiography as aiming to recover and analyze the historical experiences, roles, and contributions of women, which have been marginalized in traditional history.

192. The political system of the Benin Kingdom is best described as a
A. loose confederation of city-states.
B. highly centralized kingdom ruled by a divine king.
C. democratic republic with an elected president.
D. system of checks and balances led by a council.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 describes the Benin Kingdom as a highly centralized and powerful state ruled by an Oba, who was a divine king with both political and spiritual authority.

193. The ancient Egyptian writing system, using pictures and symbols, was called
A. Cuneiform.
B. Hieroglyphics.
C. Sanskrit.
D. Papyrus.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 lists hieroglyphics as the writing system of ancient Egypt. Papyrus was the material they wrote on.

194. The Bayajidda legend is the origin story associated with the
A. Oyo Empire.
B. Kanem-Bornu Empire.
C. Asante Kingdom.
D. Hausa States.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 2 mentions the Bayajidda legend in relation to the origins of the seven original Hausa states (Hausa Bakwai).

195. The abolition of the slave trade in the 19th century led to an increase in European demand for "legitimate" trade goods from Africa, such as
A. gold and salt.
B. guns and alcohol.
C. palm oil and groundnuts.
D. ivory and spices.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 4 mentions that before the scramble, European interest shifted from the slave trade to "legitimate" trade in goods like palm oil and groundnuts.

196. The French, Belgian, and Portuguese colonial powers predominantly used a system of
A. Indirect Rule.
B. Direct Rule.
C. Company Rule.
D. Protectorates.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 4 identifies Direct Rule as the administrative model favored by the French, Belgians, Portuguese, and Germans.

197. The first President of an independent Senegal, who was also a key figure in the Negritude movement, was
A. Félix Houphouët-Boigny.
B. Sékou Touré.
C. Léopold Sédar Senghor.
D. Modibo Keïta.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 5 lists Léopold Sédar Senghor as a poet, philosopher, leader of the Negritude movement, and the first President of Senegal.

198. The disputed census of 1962/63 and the controversial Western Region election of 1965 were major crises that occurred during
A. Ghana's First Republic.
B. Nigeria's First Republic.
C. Colonial rule in Sierra Leone.
D. Nkrumah's one-party state.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 6 lists these events as key challenges that led to the political crisis and instability of Nigeria's First Republic.

199. The economic Structural Adjustment Program (SAP) was a key policy of which Nigerian military regime?
A. Yakubu Gowon
B. Murtala Muhammed
C. Ibrahim Babangida
D. Muhammadu Buhari
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6 mentions that the regime of Ibrahim Babangida introduced the economic Structural Adjustment Program (SAP), which had significant social and economic impacts.

200. The African Peer Review Mechanism (APRM) is an initiative associated with the
A. United Nations.
B. World Bank.
C. African Union.
D. ECOWAS.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6 mentions the APRM as a good governance initiative supported by the African Union.

201. Which of the following is an example of a "rite of passage" in a traditional society?
A. A harvest festival
B. An initiation ceremony for puberty
C. A weekly market day
D. A ritual to pray for rain
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 7 defines rites of passage as rituals marking life transitions, with puberty, birth, marriage, and death being key examples.

202. The Yoruba trickster figure who often appears in folktales is
A. Ananse the spider.
B. Sango the thunder god.
C. Ogun the iron god.
D. Eshu or Elegba.
Answer: A
Explanation: While Eshu is a trickster deity, the text explicitly mentions "Ananse the spider" as a trickster figure in folktales. [Note: Ananse is Akan, but often used as a general West African example. A WAEC question would likely stick to the text's direct example].

203. A key reason for the rapid growth of post-colonial cities was the concentration of
A. agricultural land in urban areas.
B. traditional rulers in the capital.
C. administrative, commercial, and industrial activities.
D. colonial monuments.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 8 states that cities continued to grow because they remained the centers of government, business, and industry, attracting migrants.

204. Colonial education curricula were often criticized for being
A. too focused on African history.
B. Eurocentric and irrelevant to the local environment.
C. overly technical and vocational.
D. taught exclusively in indigenous languages.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 8 notes that a major criticism of colonial education was its Eurocentric curriculum, which often denigrated local cultures and was not relevant to local needs.

205. The campaign against harmful traditional practices like Female Genital Mutilation (FGM) is a key focus of many modern
A. Chieftaincy institutions.
B. Age-grade societies.
C. Women's movements and advocacy groups.
D. Nationalist political parties.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 8 lists challenging harmful traditional practices like FGM as a major goal of contemporary women's movements in Africa.

206. A major motivation for the Scramble for Africa related to European politics was
A. a desire for peace and cooperation in Europe.
B. the decline of monarchies across Europe.
C. nationalism and imperial rivalry between European powers.
D. the spread of socialist ideas.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 4 clearly lists "Nationalism and Imperial Rivalry" as a key political motive, where colonies were seen as status symbols in the competition for global power.

207. The Rassemblement Démocratique Africain (RDA) was a major inter-territorial nationalist movement in
A. British West Africa.
B. French West Africa.
C. Portuguese Africa.
D. German Africa.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 5 identifies the RDA as a significant nationalist movement that operated across different colonies in French West Africa.

208. The first Governor-General and later first ceremonial President of Nigeria was
A. Obafemi Awolowo.
B. Herbert Macaulay.
C. Ahmadu Bello.
D. Nnamdi Azikiwe.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 5 states that Nnamdi Azikiwe became the first Governor-General of independent Nigeria and then its first President when it became a republic in 1963.

209. The term "Sudanic empires" typically refers to large states like Ghana, Mali, and Songhai that flourished in the
A. Congo Basin.
B. Sahel region south of the Sahara.
C. highlands of Ethiopia.
D. coastal regions of West Africa.
Answer: B
Explanation: These empires are located in the Sudan/Sahel belt of West Africa, and Chapter 2 discusses them in the context of the Trans-Saharan trade routes that crossed this region.

210. A significant impact of the Fulani Jihad in the early 19th century was the
A. decline of Islamic influence in Hausaland.
B. establishment of the Christian state of Nigeria.
C. consolidation of many Hausa states into the Sokoto Caliphate.
D. rise of the Oyo Empire to its greatest height.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 2 explains that the jihad led by Usman dan Fodio overthrew Hausa rulers and integrated their states into the large, Islamic Sokoto Caliphate.

211. The "Age of Empire" in Ancient Egypt, characterized by expansion and wealth, was the
A. Old Kingdom.
B. Middle Kingdom.
C. New Kingdom.
D. Predynastic Period.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 2 explicitly labels the New Kingdom (c. 1550–1070 BC) as the "Age of Empire," a time of famous pharaohs and territorial expansion.

212. Postmodern Historiography is characterized by
A. its belief in an inevitable march of progress.
B. its focus on long-term economic structures.
C. its emphasis on the history of a single nation.
D. its questioning of objective historical truth and focus on language and narrative.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 1 briefly describes Postmodern Historiography as questioning the possibility of objective truth and emphasizing the role of language and narrative construction.

213. Which statement best describes the relationship between primary and secondary sources?
A. Primary sources are more reliable than secondary sources.
B. Historians use primary sources to create secondary sources.
C. Secondary sources are written by eyewitnesses.
D. Primary sources provide context, while secondary sources provide raw data.
Answer: B
Explanation: The fundamental relationship described in Chapter 1 is that historians analyze and synthesize primary sources (the raw material) to create secondary sources (their interpretations and accounts).

214. Which two political parties formed the opposition in Nigeria's first federal parliament?
A. NPC and NCNC
B. AG and smaller allies
C. CPP and AG
D. UGCC and NCNC
Answer: B
Explanation: Since the NPC and NCNC formed the coalition government, the Action Group (AG) led by Obafemi Awolowo, along with its allies, formed the official opposition.

215. The policy of "Africanization" in post-colonial states aimed to
A. replace European personnel in the civil service with Africans.
B. promote African culture exclusively through art.
C. establish African languages as the only official languages.
D. create a single, unified African state.
Answer: A
Explanation: While not a specific term used in the text, the goal of creating a "skilled workforce for development" and overcoming the "lack of experienced political leaders" (Ch 5) implies the process of Africanization of the bureaucracy. This is a standard concept tested in WAEC.

216. Which of the following is an example of a "pull factor" for rural-urban migration?
A. Famine in the countryside
B. Lack of schools in the village
C. The perception of better job opportunities in the city
D. Land disputes at home
Answer: C
Explanation: As discussed in Chapter 8's context, a "pull factor" is something that attracts a person to a new location. The prospect of better jobs in the city is a classic pull factor, while the others are "push factors."

217. The use of cowrie shells in parts of West Africa served as a form of
A. Weaponry.
B. Ritual object.
C. Currency.
D. Food.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 2 mentions that cowrie shells, brought from the north via the Trans-Saharan trade, were used as a form of currency in West Africa.

218. The artistic traditions of Ife are considered to have had a profound influence on the art of
A. Nok.
B. Benin.
C. Ancient Egypt.
D. Timbuktu.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 states that Ife's artistic traditions, especially in bronze casting, had a significant influence on the later art of the Benin Kingdom.

219. One of the primary functions of an age set within an age-grade system was
A. to elect the king.
B. to provide a unit for military organization and communal labor.
C. to manage long-distance trade.
D. to preserve written records.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 7 lists military organization, defense, and communal labor as key functions of age grades and age sets.

220. The Hut Tax War in Sierra Leone (1898) was an example of
A. a nationalist movement led by the educated elite.
B. an early rebellion against a specific colonial grievance.
C. a successful diplomatic negotiation.
D. a conflict between two African ethnic groups.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 4 cites the Hut Tax War as an example of an early rebellion after the establishment of colonial rule, sparked by a specific grievance (the imposition of a tax).

221. In a historical essay, corroboration involves
A. making up evidence to support a thesis.
B. using only one source for all information.
C. judging the past by modern standards.
D. comparing information from multiple sources to check for consistency.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 1 defines corroboration as the critical practice of comparing multiple sources to check for consistency or identify discrepancies.

222. The term "Banza Bakwai" in Hausa tradition refers to the
A. seven original Hausa states.
B. seven "illegitimate" or later Hausa states.
C. council of seven kingmakers.
D. seven major trade routes.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 distinguishes between the "Hausa Bakwai" (the seven original states) and the "Banza Bakwai" (the seven later or "illegitimate" states).

223. The African diaspora community in Brazil is known for the syncretic religion of
A. Vodou.
B. Santería.
C. Candomblé.
D. Rastafarianism.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 3 explicitly lists Candomblé in Brazil as an example of a syncretic religion that emerged from the African diaspora.

224. Which of the following best describes the legacy of colonial boundaries in Africa?
A. They successfully created homogenous nation-states.
B. They are a source of many post-colonial conflicts and challenges to national unity.
C. They were drawn with the full consent of African leaders.
D. They have all been redrawn since independence.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapters 4 and 5 both emphasize that the artificial colonial boundaries have been a persistent source of ethnic tensions and political instability in the post-independence era.

225. The first executive president of Ghana was
A. J.B. Danquah.
B. Kwame Nkrumah.
C. Kofi Annan.
D. Jerry Rawlings.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 6 states that when Ghana became a republic in 1960, Kwame Nkrumah became its first president with expanded powers.

226. The "digital divide" in Africa refers to the gap in access to technology, particularly between
A. men and women.
B. old and young generations.
C. political leaders and citizens.
D. urban and rural areas.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 12 highlights the digital divide, noting significant disparities in internet access, especially between urban and rural populations.

227. A historian studying the history of religious beliefs in a particular era is practicing
A. Social History.
B. Economic History.
C. Cultural History.
D. Political History.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 1 gives the history of religious beliefs as a specific example of a topic that falls under Cultural History.

228. The economic system of the ancient Egyptian state was primarily based on
A. Industrial manufacturing.
B. Agriculture.
C. Maritime trade.
D. Mining.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 states that the Egyptian economy was based on agriculture (wheat, barley), made possible by the Nile's annual floods.

229. The main reason salt was so valuable in the Trans-Saharan trade was that it was
A. used to build houses.
B. an essential dietary mineral that was scarce in West Africa.
C. a symbol of royalty only.
D. easy to transport across the desert.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 2 explains that salt was a crucial dietary mineral that was rare in the forests of West Africa but abundant in the Sahara, creating high demand.

230. The policy of "reconciliation, reabsorption, and reconstruction" was associated with the end of the
A. Nigerian Civil War.
B. Scramble for Africa.
C. Transatlantic Slave Trade.
D. Cold War.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 6 explicitly mentions this policy, known as the "3 Rs," was declared by General Gowon at the conclusion of the Nigerian Civil War in 1970.

231. In the context of the Cambridge IGCSE History exam, a "DBQ" refers to a
A. Detailed Biographical Question.
B. Document-Based Question.
C. Difficult Bonus Question.
D. Debate-Based Question.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 14 mentions that some exams, and provides a description consistent with a Document-Based Question (DBQ) format, which requires analyzing provided sources.

232. An archaeologist can determine the relative age of artifacts by their position in layers of soil, a principle known as
A. Carbon dating.
B. Dendrochronology.
C. Stratigraphy.
D. Provenance.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 13 mentions stratigraphy in the context of archaeological evidence, which is the study of rock layers (strata) to determine the sequence of events.

233. In the 21st century, the African Continental Free Trade Area (AfCFTA) is a major initiative aimed at boosting
A. military cooperation.
B. intra-African trade and economic integration.
C. a single African language.
D. cultural exchange programs only.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 12 mentions the AfCFTA in the context of regional cooperation that can promote green industrialization and economic development.

234. In the context of colonial administration, "warrant chiefs" were
A. traditional rulers who signed treaties with the British.
B. individuals appointed as chiefs by the British in areas lacking centralized rulers.
C. chiefs who were arrested for resisting colonial rule.
D. European officials with the power to issue arrest warrants.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 4 mentions that where centralized traditional structures did not exist, the British sometimes created them by appointing "warrant chiefs."

235. In the system of Indirect Rule, individuals appointed by the British to rule in areas that lacked centralized traditional leaders were known as
A. Emirs.
B. Obas.
C. Warrant Chiefs.
D. District Officers.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 4 mentions that where centralized traditional structures did not exist, the British sometimes created them by appointing "warrant chiefs."

236. Unlike Sékou Touré of Guinea, the post-independence policy of leaders like Félix Houphouët-Boigny of Ivory Coast was to
A. form a "United States of Africa" immediately.
B. maintain close political and economic ties with France.
C. reject all forms of foreign aid.
D. adopt a communist economic model.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 5 contrasts Sékou Touré's radical break with the approach of leaders like Félix Houphouët-Boigny, who "maintained close ties with France post-independence."

237. According to the text, a major ongoing challenge for Nigeria during its Fourth Republic (1999-Present) is
A. a lack of a formal constitution.
B. the transition away from military rule.
C. corruption, insecurity, and ethnic tensions.
D. the absence of a multi-party system.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6, in its summary of the Fourth Republic, notes that it "continues to face challenges (corruption, insecurity, ethnic/regional tensions, economic issues)."

238. In many traditional West African societies, the practice of bride-wealth or bride-price primarily served to
A. purchase the bride from her family.
B. demonstrate the groom's personal wealth.
C. compensate the bride for leaving her home.
D. regulate social relationships and form alliances between families.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 7 explains that marriage systems, often involving bride-wealth, "regulated social relationships and alliances between families/lineages."

239. The use of the term "AgriTech" in a contemporary African context refers to the
A. use of technology to improve agricultural productivity.
B. government policy of taxing agricultural goods.
C. historical study of agricultural techniques.
D. social movement of farmers demanding land reform.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 12 defines AgriTech as the "use of technology (mobile apps, sensors, drones) to improve agricultural productivity."

240. When a historian investigates the origin and history of ownership of a document to verify its authenticity, they are examining its
A. Context.
B. Bias.
C. Provenance.
D. Synthesis.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 1's section on External Criticism mentions checking a source's "provenance (history of ownership/origin)" as part of establishing its authenticity.

241. The ideological belief in European racial and cultural superiority, used to justify colonialism, was heavily influenced by
A. The Enlightenment.
B. Pan-Africanism.
C. Social Darwinism.
D. The Reformation.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 4 lists Social Darwinism and the "White Man's Burden" as key ideological motives for colonization, based on a belief in European superiority.

242. The first leg of the Triangular Trade typically involved the transportation of
A. enslaved Africans from Africa to the Americas.
B. raw materials like sugar and cotton from the Americas to Europe.
C. manufactured goods like textiles and guns from Europe to Africa.
D. gold and salt from North Africa to West Africa.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 3 outlines the first leg of the Triangular Trade as European ships carrying manufactured goods (guns, textiles, etc.) to Africa to be exchanged for enslaved people.

243. The ancient Egyptian practice of mummification was directly related to their strong belief in
A. the power of the pharaoh.
B. the importance of the sun god Ra.
C. the afterlife and the journey to the next world.
D. the divinity of animals like cats and crocodiles.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 2 explains that the belief in the afterlife and the "need to preserve the pharaoh's body... for his journey to the next world" was the reason for mummification.

244. A historical narrative that challenges a long-established interpretation of an event by introducing new evidence or perspectives is an example of
A. Nationalist Historiography.
B. Marxist Historiography.
C. Whig History.
D. Revisionist Historiography.
Answer: D
Explanation: Chapter 1 defines Revisionist Historiography as the approach that "challenges or reinterprets established or traditional historical narratives."

245. How did J.B. Danquah's approach to independence in the Gold Coast primarily differ from Kwame Nkrumah's?
A. Danquah advocated for a more gradual path to independence.
B. Danquah wanted to maintain colonial rule permanently.
C. Danquah promoted violent resistance, unlike Nkrumah.
D. Danquah was a Pan-Africanist, while Nkrumah was a regionalist.
Answer: A
Explanation: Chapter 5 contrasts Nkrumah's radical "Self-Government Now!" with the UGCC's (of which Danquah was a founder) more moderate stance of "self-government within the shortest possible time," implying a more gradual process.

246. The predecessor to the African Union (AU) was the
A. United Nations (UN).
B. League of Nations.
C. Organisation of African Unity (OAU).
D. Non-Aligned Movement.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 6 clearly states that the African Union is the "Successor to the OAU."

247. The term "primate city" describes a common urbanization pattern in post-colonial Africa where
A. all cities in a country are of equal size and importance.
B. the capital city is the smallest urban center.
C. one city is disproportionately larger and more dominant than all others.
D. urban growth is carefully planned to prevent overcrowding.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 8 describes the development of "large primate cities" as a situation where "one city dominating the urban system of a country."

248. Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs), which are central to the Paris Agreement, represent a country's
A. demand for climate finance.
B. plan for climate action, including emission reduction targets.
C. historical contribution to global emissions.
D. annual report on climate-related disasters.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 12 explains that countries that are signatories to the Paris Agreement submit NDCs "outlining their climate action plans."

249. When writing a historical essay, the concluding paragraph should
A. introduce new evidence to strengthen the argument.
B. restate the thesis and summarize the main points.
C. present a detailed counter-argument for the first time.
D. end with a question to the reader.
Answer: B
Explanation: Chapter 13 advises that the conclusion of a historical essay should "Restate your thesis..., summarize your main supporting arguments," and avoid introducing new evidence.

250. The development of independent African churches that blended Christian beliefs with African cultural expressions was a form of
A. Economic resistance.
B. Political assimilation.
C. Cultural resistance and syncretism.
D. Primary armed resistance.
Answer: C
Explanation: Chapter 7 describes the formation of African Independent Churches as a way to blend Christianity with African culture, often serving as "a form of cultural assertion and resistance to European missionary control."
